- **Limits stated.** Nothing creates links by itself: planners, the contract writer and the review requester must call `link` (the chat interpreter has no `decision` intent yet, so the owner's chat words reach `record` through a caller that passes the turn ID). Work that was never linked is not blocked by a correction, only told through its project-level context. The project's very first record racing an admission is not ordered (there is no record yet to check). An owner conflict is shown in the context record and sent as an event, but not yet as an owner notification (U11's kinds are tied to records; a `decision_dispute` blocker or notification kind would be the way). Per decision at most 200 tasks may rely on it, 50 links per task, 500 follow-ups per task; unfinished propagations are queued without limit in practice (the project's context record holds their IDs, about 36 bytes each). The "suspended" agent is the paused task (L01's pause gate), which this tests; the pause service's own `preparing-to-suspend`/`suspended` states are not separate here. Branch `resume` does not consult the guard.
- **For later cards.** H12 and any dispatcher must pass `GenerationService.guardFor(taskId)` (which now includes this guard) to anything that starts an effect, and call `resume` at start-up. The deps object of `DecisionService` is read when used, so the generation service (which takes the guard) can be set after both exist. A planner that writes plans and contracts should `link` them in the same step.
- **Review round 1 changes.** (a) For a chat source the phrase is judged in the whole sentence(s) of the owner's message that hold it (`notADecisionIn`): question marks, hypothetical and modal markers (what if, should we, could we, maybe, I wonder, if we ...) and a negation anywhere before the phrase (do not, never, no longer ...) record nothing, so quoting half a sentence no longer works. (b) `revalidate` checks each link by what it is: a plan, contract, action, branch or task link needs the owner or `attempt.work` on the task; a review link needs the owner or a reviewer (`review.decide`) who did not request it. `subjects` names the links to clear; without it the caller clears what it may and `remaining` counts the rest, so a worker and a reviewer each clear their own. A reviewer who does not work on the task clears nothing else. (c) `link` and `revalidate` accept only the decision `authoritative` returns for the task and topic (a project-level decision a task-level one overrides is refused). (d) Versions are now topic-wide, and `basedOn` is compared with the newest live decision that applies to the scope, task-level ones included for a project-wide correction, so a stale session gets a dispute, not a silent supersede. (e) The guard checks the gate topic and each linked decision's revision (not the whole context record), so unrelated decisions never make an admission retry. (f) `resume` and `propagate` need the owner or a host/service holding `assignment.manage` in the project, `propagation` reads need `assignment.read`. (g) The owner's correction is never refused for a backlog: the propagation is queued, and `resume` does at most 20 a call (`more` says to call again). (h) Tests for uncertain and started effect follow-ups.
- **Review round 2 changes.** A conflict now involves every live decision in the new one's scope plus the newest that applies to it (a stale project-wide correction disputes the project-level decision and all task-level exceptions on the topic; at most 50 members), and all of them read as disputed and block linked work until the owner chooses. `resolveDispute` keeps the chosen decision and supersedes every other member: choosing the newcomer leaves it the only live decision everywhere; choosing a task-level one leaves the rest of the project with no decision on the topic until the owner makes one. `authoritative` and the gate guard take the newest live entry that applies to the task, not the first match. The project's first record of any kind also creates the empty gate topic, so a gate decision made later is ordered against admissions; only the project's very first record racing an admission is not ordered (nothing exists to check).
- **Lead decisions recorded.** `planning-only` allows planning (a generation may begin) but refuses effects, the card's "admits no work" read as no implementation work; `implementation-hold` refuses a new generation too. A project-wide correction supersedes task-level exceptions on the same topic. Unlinked work is not blocked by a correction: links are created by the planners, so H12, the contract writer and the review requester must call `link`; this is the biggest practical gap and is recorded for the owner. A task's own agent may revalidate its own links, but the effect guard still needs the linked decision to be the authoritative one.
- **Mutation checks.** Each of these fails tests: a non-owner may record; chat wording unchecked; hypotheticals recorded; a task-level decision superseding everything; a stale `basedOn` ignored; `basedOn` not required; stale links not blocking; disputed links not blocking; revalidation accepting a superseded decision; link accepting a superseded decision; planning-only not stopping effects; the hold not stopping a generation; no follow-up for finished effects; nobody notified; no generation interruption; progress not saved (tasks told twice); anyone resolving a dispute; an agent accepting a follow-up; a requester re-approving its own review; the review and generation guards switched off; the guard's revision checks dropped (race test); superseded wording not marked; the same statement recorded again. Survivors, judged equivalent or untested: the early "already decided" return in `ask` (the check inside the command gives the same answer), the context's own state filter (the active list never holds a superseded ID), the retrieval digest's state field (the `supersededBy` field changes it too), and the task-credential project check in `canReadDecision` (no test with a task credential).


## C06 notes: inference-cache adapters with per-backend tuning (2026-10-22)

`packages/controller/src/inference-cache/` (`profile.ts` types, validation, negotiation; `profiles.ts` the eight profiles; `adapter.ts` key derivation, slot affinity, the adapter and its fallback; `markers.ts` `MarkerService`), 94 tests (`profiles.test.ts` 19, `adapter.test.ts` 19, `markers.test.ts` 14 on each of four storage backends) plus one new test on each backend in `budgets/budgets.test.ts`. New id prefix `cmk` (cache marker). Pure TypeScript, no new dependency, no network use: tests run against simulated servers (llama.cpp slots, vLLM salted blocks, a Claude-style breakpoint cache with lifetimes) and response shapes derived from each backend's source or documentation (not captured from running servers). Nothing was sent to any real server.

- **Profile is chosen by configured `kind`, never guessed.** `profileFor({ kind, model, options })` looks only at `kind` (one of `llama-cpp`, `vllm`, `radiance`, `strata`, `openai`, `openai-compatible`, `claude`, `generic`; exact match). No kind, or a kind this code does not know, is the **generic** profile: it sends nothing, expects nothing, reports every capability unsupported and reads a standard hit field only if the response happens to carry one. A model called `claude-...` or `gpt-...` with no kind is generic (tested). The model name is carried but used for one lookup only: Claude's minimum prefix length once the kind already says Claude. Owner-confirmed facts about the server (`slots`, `slotSave`, `promptDetails`, `prefixCaching`, `promptCacheKey`, `extendedRetention`, `pinPrefix`, `hostTier`, `hybrid`, `minPrefixTokens`) are endpoint `options`; an unknown option name is refused. Nothing in R01's routing table is changed: the dispatcher keeps the endpoint config next to the route and passes it in.
- **Capabilities are separate answers.** Each profile declares `prefixReuse` (automatic, markers or none), `retention` (none, request or server, with a maximum where there is one), `isolation` (request salt or none; OpenAI's `prompt_cache_key` is a routing hint, not a partition, so OpenAI is `none`), and `pin`, `fork`, `decoderResume`, `slotSaveRestore` each as unsupported, available or qualified, with how or why not. Summary (all `available` = read from source or documentation, **not measured**; nothing is `qualified` until G10 runs, a test enforces it): llama.cpp pin/fork/slot files available when `--slot-save-path` is configured; Strata pin (`strata_prefix`, needs `--prompt-cache 3`+), fork and slot 0 session files; vLLM, Radiance, OpenAI, Claude fork only (a shared prefix), no pin; **decoder resume unsupported everywhere** (backend-protocol-audit.md: unproven). `negotiate(profile, wants)` returns what is granted, what is not and what happens instead (the call carries its full context and runs without the cache), and which grants are unqualified.
- **What each profile sends and reads.** llama.cpp: `cache_prompt: true` and `id_slot` (a slot per agent or shared group, from `SlotAffinity`, an in-memory LRU partitioned by project: a project gives up only its own groups and is refused, not given, another project's slot), hits `timings.cache_n` / `prompt_n`. vLLM: `cache_salt` = the project's derived scope key (projects never share blocks; agents of one project do), hits `usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`, which exists only with `--enable-prompt-tokens-details`, so without it hits are *unknown*, not zero. Radiance: `session_id` label only (its source lists `cache_prompt`, `id_slot` and `prompt_cache_key` as accepted and ignored, so they are not sent); hits from `timings.cache_n` / `prompt_n` (fallback `usage.prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`) with the **hybrid caveat** (a hit may count attention blocks while every linear layer was re-run; check `/sessions` or the prompt time); host tier, TTL, checkpoint slots and the 8-sequence limit are tuning notes. Strata (read from main at 011c9e6, which reports 0.1.47, against the installed 0.1.43): `strata_checkpoint: false` for one-shot calls, `strata_prefix: {messages: N}` to pin a shared document or preamble (one pin per engine), slot 0 session files; newer-than-0.1.43 settings (`--prompt-cache-spread`, `--conversation-cache-park-gain`) are marked `since`; its answer shape is llama.cpp's `timings` plus `cached_tokens`. OpenAI: `prompt_cache_key` per sharing group (the derived share key, not a session), `prompt_cache_retention: "24h"` only for models the owner marked `extendedRetention`, 1,024-token minimum (a zero under it is expected, not a miss), hits from `prompt_tokens_details` or the Responses shape `input_tokens_details`. OpenAI-compatible: the same but conservative, nothing is sent unless the owner confirmed it (key only with `promptCacheKey`, never retention), so a strict server is not sent fields it may refuse. Claude: up to four `cache_control` breakpoints (end of system or tools, optionally per-message, last message), `ttl` 5m or 1h with the 1h entries kept first (a 1h after a 5m is lowered and recorded), a table of per-model minimum prefix lengths (an unlisted model assumes 4096 so no breakpoint is wasted), existing markers replaced, thinking blocks never marked; hits `cache_read_input_tokens` with input = `input_tokens` + read + created, writes split by lifetime.
- **Never the guess.** Every `prepare` is pure (input untouched, deterministic, `plain: true` removes every cache setting) and reports what it `applied`, what it `skipped` and why, and whether a hit is `expected` (yes, maybe, no). `judge` turns a response into `hit`, `partial`, `miss` (a hit was expected, nothing served), `cold` or `unknown`.
- **Fallback without context loss.** `CacheAdapter.call` sends the prepared request; if the endpoint answers 400 with wording that names a cache setting (an unknown field, a refused marker or lifetime), the **same messages** are sent again with every cache setting removed, and the result records what was removed and why. Other errors (overflow, 5xx, 429) are returned untouched for R02 to classify; the profile's own `neverCall` list (`/reset_prefix_cache`, vLLM `/pause` and `/resume`) is refused by the adapter whatever path is configured, because those empty every agent's cache. Slot save and restore (`CacheAdapter.slot`) never throw on a server refusal: they return `{ ok: false, reason }`. Save and restore take the **slot-file marker the dispatcher has just resolved for the project** (the adapter cannot check ownership; resolving first is the dispatcher's duty), and the file name comes from the marker (`nn-<20 chars>.bin`, controller-generated), never from a caller. A refusal is recognised only by the field we sent as a whole word (`id_slot`, `cache_prompt`, `cache_salt`, `session_id`, `strata_prefix`, `prompt_cache_key`, `cache_control`, `ttl`, ...) in a 400, or in a 422 whose body (FastAPI `detail`/`loc`) names it; words like "little" or `n_ctx_slot` do not count. On Claude, `cache_control` is removed only from tools, system blocks, top-level message content blocks and tool_result content blocks, never from `input_schema`, a `tool_use` input or text, so the model sees the same data on the plain fallback.
- **Keys are derived, not chosen.** `deriveKey(secret, "scope" | "share", ...parts)` is an HMAC of the parts under an installation secret (at least 16 characters, supplied by the caller from configuration), `nn-` plus 27 base64url characters. Cache keys, salts and labels carry only this text: a cloud provider never sees a project or task ID, and without the secret another project's key cannot be computed. The scope key (per project) is the isolation boundary; the share key (per project, meeting or common preamble) is what C06b should use so that agents that can share a prefix do.
- **Opaque markers, bounded retention, quota.** `MarkerService` (owner, host or service only; agents never see a marker, digest or file name) records `{id, route, backend, kind (prefix, slot-file, pin), digest of the prefix, tokens, expiry}`. A marker is only made for something the endpoint's profile advertises (a slot file needs slot save configured, a pin needs a backend that has one); its expiry never exceeds the profile's own maximum (Claude: one hour) or seven days; a project holds at most 200 live markers and 50 million tokens, checked in the same guarded write that adds the marker (ten concurrent registrations for five places give five; tested); released markers and stale prefix or pin markers are swept first; **stale slot-file markers (expired or lost) are kept until released**, because they are the only record of the file the controller made on the server, count against a 400-record cap but not the quota, and `pendingErase` lists their handles so the dispatcher erases the file and then releases the marker; nothing is evicted silently (the caller releases something). A marker's default expectation is the backend's real lifetime (`defaultLifetimeMs`: 5 minutes for Claude's 5m breakpoint and OpenAI in-memory, 24 hours with extended retention, an hour for servers), and an endpoint config that names its `route` must match the marker's route. `resolve` says active (with the tokens to expect) or unavailable (missing, expired, released, lost); another project's marker or another route reads like a missing one. `recordOutcome` (a credential-shaped reason is refused, as in the neighbouring services) counts hits and turns a **miss into lost**, so later calls stop expecting tokens the backend no longer holds. All commands are idempotent by command ID.
- **Usage ledger (R05) edit.** `BudgetService.settle` and `charge` take an optional `cache: { backend, route, cachedTokens, computedTokens, writtenTokens }`, kept on the ledger entry and summed in `usage()` as `cache` and `cacheByBackend`. For Claude `writtenTokens` is a subset of `computedTokens` (tokens computed and then written to the cache), not an addition. Entries without it are not counted, so unknown is never added as zero; tokens never mix with the cost or compute figures. `ledgerCache(hit, backend, route)` builds the object from a `CacheHit` (undefined when the response carried no figure).
- **Limits stated.** (1) Nothing here is measured on a real backend; `qualified` stays unused until G10 runs, and the Claude 512-token minimums, OpenAI's roughly 15 requests a minute per key and prefix, and the Radiance and Strata flag behaviour are read from documentation or source and must be confirmed there. (2) The adapters do not do HTTP, authentication, retries or failover: the dispatcher supplies a `Transport` (credentials per route) and R02 handles provider failures; a cache refusal fallback is the only retry made here. (3) Slot hygiene (llama.cpp, the one backend where a call can name a slot): with slots configured, a call that names no slot (none given, or `SlotAffinity` refused) is sent with `cache_prompt: false`, because the server would place it by prompt similarity, possibly in another project's slot. The adapter remembers which project last held each slot; a call into a slot whose holder is another project or **unknown** (every slot after a start, a release, or any slot-less call) first clears that slot (`/slots/N?action=erase`; `freshSlot: "erase" | "off"`, default erase; if the clear fails the call runs with reuse off). A restart or a release-then-reassign therefore costs one re-read and cannot reuse another project's KV; two adapters for one server each start with no knowledge and clear on first use. `clearSlot` clears the in-memory KV only and **does not delete any saved file**: llama.cpp and Strata have no API for that, so the files in `pendingErase` are deleted by a host-side job by exact name from `--slot-save-path` (Strata: its session-file directory, same check), after which the marker is released. Slot affinity itself is in memory and per project. **Shared per server:** the holder map, the affinity and a per-slot turn live in an `EndpointState` that the controller owns through one `CacheEndpoints` registry (`state(url)`, URL normalised) and passes to every adapter of that server (`options.state`); an adapter without one has private state and is correct only as the server's sole user. Calls into one slot take turns (a clear and a send of different projects never interleave); a call naming no slot takes the whole server's turn; different slots run side by side. A refused `id_slot` is retried with `cache_prompt: false` (llama.cpp defaults to reuse and would otherwise place the retry by similarity), every holder is then forgotten (the retry went to an unknown slot), and a refusal naming `cache_prompt` itself is returned as an error, not retried. (4) llama.cpp save and restore wait for an idle slot and cannot snapshot a running decode; slot files are specific to the model and build; Strata has one slot (0). (5) Prompt layout (stable-first ordering, what Pi renders first, volatile content out of system sections, compaction effects) is **C06b**; this card only offers the `scope` and `share` keys, the per-backend settings and the hit readings it needs, and strips and re-places Claude markers itself (pi-ai places its own; C06b decides who owns placement). (6) Not in the state API, no outbox or notification, and no wiring into the dispatcher (H12) or the fallback service (R02): the dispatcher is expected to pick the route (R01), `begin` the call (R02), `prepare` and `call` through the profile, `resolve` and `recordOutcome` the marker, and `settle` with `ledgerCache(...)`. (7) Radiance's `/sessions` and `/stats` are not read (no adapter method); the hybrid caveat is the only guard against a hit figure that overstates reuse. (8) Pi's thinking blocks and Anthropic's thinking-block rules (edited history invalidates them) are C06b's.

- **Isolation.** `CacheContext.isolationRequired` (set by the dispatcher for a project that requires it): on a backend whose isolation is `none` the call carries no cache settings (llama.cpp is sent `cache_prompt: false`), expects nothing, and the result's `skipped` says why; the server's own cache may still run on Radiance, Strata, OpenAI and Claude, so such a project should be routed to vLLM (salted) or to a dedicated instance. Strata's single pin is a **durable route-level record** (`cache-pin`), checked in the same guarded write as the pin marker: another project's live pin on the route is refused, the holder may replace its own, and release, loss (a miss) or expiry frees it; it survives a restart and a second adapter, and the adapter sends `strata_prefix` only when the dispatcher says (`pinAuthorized`) it holds the pin marker for the project, so an unknown holder counts as someone else.
- **Lead decisions to be put to the owner later.** (1) The cross-project timing side channel on shared local backends and on Claude's organization-wide cache is accepted by default for a single-owner installation and removed per project by `isolationRequired`. (2) C06b decides who owns Claude marker placement; the adapter's strip-and-replace is acceptable until then. (3) Quota overflow is refused, not evicted. (4) Claude's 512-token minimums are unverified; G10 verifies. (5) Endpoint configs live with the dispatcher next to the R01 route, and `register` refuses an endpoint whose named route differs.
- **G10 follow-up.** The response shapes in the tests are derived from source and documentation, not captured. G10 should capture one real response per backend (llama.cpp timings, vLLM with `--enable-prompt-tokens-details`, Radiance timings and usage on a hybrid model, Strata 0.1.43 and latest, OpenAI chat and Responses, Claude with 5m and 1h writes), replace the fixtures with them, and only then mark capabilities `qualified`.
- **Strata and the timing signal.** Strata's checkpoints, parked conversations and pin are engine-wide and it has no per-request reuse switch, so `isolationRequired` can only keep our settings off (`strata_checkpoint: false`, no pin); **only a route of its own isolates on Strata, Radiance and Claude** (Claude's cache is organization-wide). Lead decision: the cross-project timing signal on shared non-isolating backends is accepted by default for a single-owner installation and closed by `isolationRequired` (vLLM salted, llama.cpp reuse off, a route of its own for the rest); the slot partition (project-owned slots, clear on hand-over, reuse off without a slot) is nevertheless real as claimed, for llama.cpp.

## M03 notes: stand-up preparation, briefs and agenda items (2026-10-23)

`packages/controller/src/standups/` (`StandupService`, `ImprovementService`, `shared.ts`), changes to `meetings/meetings.ts` (M01), the state API (`agenda.capture`, `agenda.correct`, `agenda.get`, `agenda.list`; `docs/nimblenewt/api-v1.md` regenerated) and the chat (`agenda.capture` and `agenda.correct` as chat operations), one new test harness field (`meetings`). Tests: `standups/standups.test.ts` (19 tests) and `chat/agenda.test.ts` (5 tests), each on all four backends. Mutation-checked (list in the report). The meeting procedure (M05, M06) is not built and still waits for the owner's acceptance of the M00 record.

- **Stand-up configuration.** `configure` makes a daily M01 schedule (a recurrence can be given) and a `standup-config` record named by the schedule: the participant group is the schedule's roster, plus length, zone (the schedule's own or the owner's `meeting.timeZone`), advance notice (`prepareMinutes`, default 60), **the daily window** (kept on the schedule itself as well: M01's `createSchedule`/`updateSchedule` refuse any change of time, length or window that leaves it, also when called directly; lead decision) and the coverage of a brief (hours, default 24, at most 168). *Decision:* "daily window" is read as the hours of the local day in which the stand-up may be held: it must start at or after `windowFrom` and end (start plus length) by `windowTo`, with no window across midnight. `update` re-checks the rule on the values the stand-up will have, then changes the schedule (M01 rules: the cursor moves, the past is not replayed) and the config. A refused set-up creates nothing (the window is checked before the schedule is made); a repeat of the same command returns the same stand-up. DST and the single catch-up report are M01's tick, tested here together with preparation (09:00 Berlin is 07:00 UTC before the autumn change and 08:00 after it; four days of downtime make one `reported` catch-up occurrence with briefs and no notices).
- **Advance notice.** `prepare(by, occurrence)` (owner, host or service only) hands one notice per reachable agent to an injected `PreparationSink` with the stable key `<occurrence>:<agent>:prepare`: "reach a safe boundary and save your position; your brief is put together from the records". Availability is asked again every time (it fails closed: a missing or throwing hook withholds everyone and records `stopped`); `available` agents are told, a paused agent is told only if the owner invited it by hand (M01 `owner-invited`), suspended and stopped agents never. Nothing resumes, starts or wakes anything (tested: the pause is still in force and the sink saw only the available agent). A notice is recorded (`standup-prep`) only after the sink returned, so a failed delivery is retried with the same key; an agent resumed later is told by the next preparation. A catch-up report and a meeting that has ended get no notice.
- **Briefs.** `standup-brief`, one per occurrence and agent, made by `prepare` for every participant, paused ones included, from the records alone: *accomplishments* (the agent's tasks marked done in the period, artifacts recorded for them), *unfinished work* (ready, in progress, blocked), *blockers* (open blockers and waits of those tasks; a blocked task with none says so), *advice needed* (the agent's open inquiries) and *next priorities* (unblocked unfinished tasks by contract priority, five at most). The period is from the previous handled meeting (`coversFrom`, which a catch-up carries) or the coverage hours before the start, to the preparation time. **Every entry carries its citations** (kind, ID, revision of the record) and nothing is written without one. Entries are 25 per section at most, the rest counted in `more`. A scan cut by its budget (`scanPages` x `scanPageSize`, 25 x 200) marks the brief and the review `partial`. The brief is refreshed when the records change (a new revision, same ID) and left as the meeting saw it once the live meeting has started; a repeat with nothing changed writes nothing. The agent may add its own words (`addStatement`: advice, priorities or a note, ten at most, only to its own brief, only before the start); they are kept apart from the record-backed sections and shown as the agent's own.
- **Who reads a brief (lead decision).** The owner, host or service, the agent it is about, managers of the project's meetings (`meeting.manage`), and holders of both `meeting.read` and `history.read`; the last two see the record-backed sections only. The words and recipient of a question an agent asked another (`detail` of an advice entry) go to the owner, the controller and that agent only, because the inquiry's own rule lets only its parties and the owner read it; everyone else gets the citation and "asked another agent a question". The planning review names tasks and follows the same rule as the brief. The owner's to-do list (`needsOwner`) goes only to the owner, a host or a service, in `prepared()` and in `agenda()`; items of the notification inbox that name no project are never shown on a project's agenda. Anyone else gets what a missing brief gives.
- **The agenda.** `agenda(viewer, occurrence)` returns the points in meeting order and grouped: urgent points first (inherited from M01, lead decision), then **conflicts, questions, decisions**, then other topics, then improvement proposals, oldest first within each. M01's agenda items gained `category` (derived when absent: a delegated decision or instruction is a decision, an item with a question text is a question, otherwise a topic; a `conflict` may be recorded by the owner or a manager only, so an agent that disagrees raises a question), `timeboxMinutes` and a `link` to an improvement proposal. The same order is used by `openAgenda` and by the items a meeting takes (so the first 25 are the right 25). Each line states **what its request type lets the team do** (`authorityOf`): a discussion or a recommendation request executes nothing and its outcome goes back as findings; a delegated decision may be decided within existing authority; an owner instruction is planned, not voted on. The planning section is the P/PC review (below); briefs are listed if the viewer may read them, otherwise `withheld` says so. Routine progress stays in the briefs.
- **Supervisor-captured items.** `agenda.capture` through the state API and the chat: the item is linked to the owner's chat turn (the controller sets `sourceTurnId`; an interpreter that names one is refused), the topic, question and context must be the owner's own words (the chat's existing rule for every text), and the answer shows the item back in plain lines (topic, "your question, as recorded", context, outcome asked for, urgency, who it is for, request type and its authority, "nothing has been done on it", the ID and revision). `agenda.correct` fixes it by ID or by words in its topic (exactly one open item of the project, else the owner is asked which; the revision is filled in); the corrections are counted and the fields kept (ten). Only the owner can set or change an instruction or a decision delegated to the team, and a correction by anyone else cannot touch an item that is one; in chat such a capture is a **proposal** the owner applies in a later message, and quoted material makes any capture a proposal. The API accepts `sourceTurnId` from the owner only (the chat sets it itself). The default request type is `discussion`; a tentative idea never becomes an instruction through a paraphrase.
- **States, owner and outcome links.** M01's queued, scheduled, discussed, deferred and resolved states, with owner, `outcomeRef` and (new) `outcomeNote` in words. A transition to discussed, resolved or deferred of an item that came from a conversation (chat turn or inquiry) queues its outcome in a per-project list in the same commit (`agenda-returns`: 200 listed, then an overflow list of 1000, both configurable in `MeetingOptions`). **A full list never blocks a state change:** the outcome goes on the overflow list and the item is marked `returnUnlisted`; `requeueReturns` lists it when there is room (done by `deliverOutcomes`); the next agenda says how many outcomes wait. Only when both lists are full is an outcome on neither: the state change is still recorded, the item stays marked `returnUnlisted` and a held meeting counts it in `returnsUnlisted`, but nothing finds it again (that needs 1200 undelivered outcomes in one project, i.e. no delivery loop); a held meeting queues the topics it did not reach, with their reason and next step. `deliverOutcomes` (owner, host or service) first lists outcomes that did not fit (`requeueReturns`), takes a snapshot of the waiting list (so more than 50 outcomes are fine), hands each to an injected `OutcomeSink` with the key `<item>:<revision>` and marks it returned only afterwards, and only if the item is still at the revision that was sent (a newer outcome stays waiting and goes next time; retry-safe, at least once); the text is built from the typed record, and for a discussion or recommendation it says that no work was started because of it. Deferred items stay on the open list with reason and next step.
- **P/PC balance review.** Stored with the preparation and shown on the agenda: production delivered since the period start and pending; capacity (PC) work finished and under way and the improvement proposals open, adopted and stopped; the configured allocation (floor, target, ceiling from the `workClass.*` settings via `allocationFrom`) against actual use; deferred PC work (blocked PC tasks and deferred proposals with their next steps); the next protected improvement window. Every line cites records. *Accepted by the lead as a rough guide until a usage provider exists.* *Honest limits:* actual use is the share of PC among tasks worked on or finished, **by count** and labelled as a rough guide, because worker-slot time (the accounting basis) is not measured by any card yet; an injected `usage` provider replaces it when R05 or a later card supplies one. No protected window exists as a record yet, so the line says "none is scheduled" unless an `improvementWindow` provider is given.
- **Improvement backlog.** `ImprovementService`: `propose` (anyone with `meeting.agenda`, `meeting.manage` or the owner) with observation, possible improvement, evidence (tasks, artifacts or desk records of this project that the proposer can read now, ten at most), affected tasks and a timebox (default 10, at most 60 minutes); `group` (managers) puts a repeat under the first proposal without merging: the duplicate keeps its own record and evidence and is decided with its primary; `decide` (managers): investigating, accepted (optionally with the task the P/PC process made of it), deferred (reason and next step), rejected (reason), adopted (the evaluation it was judged on is required) or stopped (reason), final ones final; `bringToStandup` puts it on the agenda as an `improvement` point with its timebox and a link, one open point at a time; `deliverOutcomes` tells every proposer of the group once. Accepting a proposal creates no task and grants nothing; implementation and publication authority stay with the P/PC process and the toolbox rules. Nothing counts proposals, so no agent has a quota to fill. At most 200 proposals are open per project (one index record; a decided proposal stays on it until its decision is told).
- **Authorization recap.** No operation takes an actor, resolver or principal from the caller. `prepare`, `deliverOutcomes`, `outcomesToReturn` and `markOutcomeReturned` are for owner, host or service (never a task credential). Configuration, grouping, deciding and bringing to the stand-up need `meeting.manage`. Reads of what the viewer may not see look like missing records.
- **Limits and what later cards must know.** (1) Nothing calls `prepare` or `deliverOutcomes` by itself: the controller's loop must (the same loop that calls `tick`), shortly after the occurrence appears and again close to the start; `prepare` is idempotent. (2) The notice sink and the outcome sink (post into the chat turn, an inquiry, an agent's inbox; or a U11 notice) are wiring for later cards. **The digest is part of the daily meeting** (owner decision 2026-10-22): `StandupOptions.ownerItems` (ready-made: `needsOwnerFrom(notifications, owner)`, unanswered inbox items of the project, urgent first, then oldest, each with its citation) is stored with the preparation and opens the agenda as `needsOwner`; a provider that fails is said to have failed, not left out. Nothing sends the digest anywhere; the Notice / Needs you / Urgent levels (U11b) and the 09:00 UTC fallback digest when no stand-up exists remain unbuilt. (3) The daily window is enforced by M01 itself on every change of a schedule that carries one. (4) Briefs read tasks, assignments, blockers, waits, inquiries and artifacts only; they do not read desk progress records or tool activity. Blockers on effects, deliveries or messages are not listed (the blocked task says so). (5) A scan reads whole kinds in ID order, bounded as in U05; an index would be needed on a very large installation. (6) `bringToStandup` called by two different managers at once can make two agenda points (the point's ID derives from the caller); the second is harmless and can be resolved. (7) The chat cannot yet refer to "the item I just made" without its words or ID. (8) M05/M06 should call `setAgendaState` with `outcomeNote`, then `deliverOutcomes`; briefs are read through `briefs(...)`.



## M04 notes: meeting attendance and return to work (2026-10-22)

`packages/controller/src/meetings/attendance.ts` (`AttendanceService`), `attendance.test.ts` (33 tests on each of the four backends). Scope is attendance and return only: the meeting procedure (chair, floor, motions, ballots, summaries, the delivery rule that a participant not holding the floor gets no further messages) is M05/M06 and waits for the owner's acceptance of the M00 record; nothing in the M00 record was changed. Mutation-checked (list in the report).

- **Records.** `meeting-room` (one per occurrence: kind `scheduled | urgent | immediate`, scope, roster, optional `requireAll`, the C01 meeting history, `returnPolicy: attendance-pauses-only`, discussion start, end), `meeting-attendance` (one per occurrence and participant: state, reason, interrupted work, meeting task, join/leave times, `retain`, return plan, saved brief, the last 50 events with an exact `eventCount`), `meeting-revalidation` (one per agent: what its meetings' decisions require before it continues), `meeting-return-pending` (one list, 200 at most, of attendances whose return waits), `meeting-room-open` (one list, 200 at most, of open rooms) and, written in `lifecycle/pause.ts`, `pause-exemption`.
- **Rooms and states.** `openRoom` (scheduled or urgent occurrence of M01; `meeting.manage` or owner/host/service) makes the room and one attendance per roster member, decided from the lifecycle at that moment: `preparing`, `unavailable` (paused: not woken by a schedule; suspended or stopped: not woken; reason says so), and `preparing` + `bounded` for a paused agent the owner invited by hand (M01 `inviteDirect`). `blocked` is shown for an agent that reported an operation it cannot safely interrupt (`reportBlocked`) or whose interrupted task's executor reports `recovery-required`/`unreachable`/an uncooperative boundary; it is derived from the gates when read, so it clears when they reach `paused`. `status` returns everything a late joiner needs (states, reasons, what each is waiting on, the merged timeline, agenda, whether the discussion may begin); a viewer who cannot see the meeting gets `undefined`.
- **Immediate meetings.** `callNow` takes a project and a scope: `{kind: "project"}` (the project's plan participants that are active agents) or `{kind: "team", teamId}` (its active agents; direct members only). No scope, an unknown team or an empty roster returns `needs-scope` with the choices and creates nothing, interrupts nobody, sends nothing. Over 50 agents returns `too-large` with the count (no truncation). Otherwise it calls M01's `callUrgent`, opens the room as `immediate` and, for owner/host/service callers, sends the high-priority invitations (a manager's room waits for one of them; invitation sending is theirs in M01). The room is open at once; unavailable agents are shown as such.
- **Everyone required.** `requireAll: {timeoutMinutes}` (1 to 1440) at room opening. While the whole roster is not present and the deadline has not passed the phase is `gathering` (deadline and missing are in `status`) and `beginDiscussion` refuses. After the deadline the phase is `open`; `beginDiscussion` records who it started with and who was missing and `fullAttendance: false`; a later arrival is `joined-late` and does not rewrite that record. Without `requireAll` the discussion may begin at once with whoever is present (at least one participant). Waiting wakes nobody; for an unavailable participant whose presence matters (everyone required, or an agenda item names it) `status` gives the owner question ("authorize waking or recovering it, or go ahead without it?").
- **Joining.** `prepare` (meeting manager or owner/host/service; the pause itself needs L01's authority, so a caller without `assignment.manage` on the task is refused) pauses the interrupted tasks (at most 5, each assigned to the agent, verified) with an ordinary task-scope L01 `pause` and records for each whether this attendance made the pause (`pausedByUs`, with the request ID) or it was already paused. `join` (the agent itself, or a meeting manager/owner/host/service) needs every interrupted task paused and observed `paused`; otherwise nothing changes and the answer says what it waits on (or that it is `blocked`). It then creates a separate meeting task in the occurrence's project (`Meeting: <title>`), assigns it to the agent, moves it to `in_progress`, and records the join. Each step is skipped if done and tolerates a concurrent identical step, so a join interrupted anywhere is finished by repeating it. The interrupted assignment, its owner, generation, attempts and background work are not written (tested by revision); only a Pause is used, never Suspend, Release, transfer or Force Stop. Background results of the paused task still record (`recordBackground`).
- **Paused agents: the exemption (change in `pause.ts`).** An agent-level (or team or installation) pause blocks every task of the agent, including a meeting task. For a paused agent the owner invited by hand, `join` writes a `pause-exemption` for that one meeting task in the same commit as the join; `PauseService#chain` then treats that task as governed by its own gate only. It is valid only while `active`, before `expiresAt` (the meeting's end plus M01's 60-minute grace, injected clock) and while the task is assigned to that agent; `leave` and the end of the meeting end it. The record is locked with the chain (a revision check), so ending it races safely. It lifts no gate: the agent stays `paused` for conversations, and every other task of the agent stays blocked. Only an owner-invited participant gets one.
- **Wake.** `authorizeWake` is the owner only, needs a composed `WakeSink` (L02/L03 supply it; without one it is refused rather than pretended), asks the sink once per `<occurrence>:<agent>`, and shows the agent `preparing`; the agent still cannot join until the lifecycle reports it reachable (`refresh` or `join` re-read it). Nothing else in this service (prepare, join, refresh, invitations) can wake an agent.
- **Late joiner and the owner.** A room runs without the owner. `joinAsOwner` records the owner's presence (no pause, no task) and may come late; `status` shows the timeline so far and the C01 meeting conversation id (opened when a `HistoryService` is composed, with the roster plus the opener as participants; the late joiner reads the entries through `HistoryService.read`).
- **Saved brief.** `saveBrief` (the agent itself, 4,000 characters, redacted-text check) and `briefOf`: always `live: false`, with the time it was saved and a notice that it is not a live answer, for present or absent agents alike. Generating the brief is M05.
- **Return to work.** `endMeeting` (owner/host/service, M01's rule for marking a meeting held) closes the room first (a join racing it is refused or has landed and is then made to leave), has everyone present leave (meeting tasks finished, exemptions ended), settles the occurrence `held` via M01, applies the decisions, and returns every attendee. `returnToWork` does the same for one agent that has left, and `sweepReturns` repeats the waiting ones. Per interrupted task: not paused by this attendance (it predated the meeting) is `kept`; `keepPaused` (owner, or whoever may pause the task: L01 refuses a second pause on a held gate, so this is how a pause requested during the meeting is kept) is `kept`; a gate that was resumed or replaced by another request (its request ID is no longer ours) is `kept`; an assignment that is no longer the same agent at the same generation is `ownership-changed` (the attendance pause is lifted so the new owner is not held, nothing is moved); a deferral that has not reached its time is `deferred`; a task a decision supersedes whose agent has not acknowledged is `revalidating`; otherwise L01's `resume` of the task gate is the only thing done (`resumed`, with `stillPausedBy` listing the agent, team or installation gates that still hold it). Agent, team and installation pauses, suspensions and stops are never touched; a suspended or stopped agent is noted in the outcome and not woken.
- **Decisions.** `endMeeting` takes up to 20 decisions (`ref`, `supersedes` task IDs, `defers` task IDs with `deferMinutes` up to 14 days, 100 tasks in all, existing tasks only, no credential-shaped text). Until M02/M06 produce decision records these are the caller's list. A superseded task's agent gets a `meeting-revalidation` entry (due in `revalidateWithinMinutes`, default 24 h, at most 7 days); an attendee's own paused task stays paused until it acknowledges; an absent agent is sent a notice through an optional `NoticeSink` (at least once, key `<occurrence>:<agent>`, `notifiedAt` recorded after the sink returned, so a failing sink is retried by repeating the end). `revalidation(taskId)` answers whether the task's current assignee still owes an acknowledgement (for the owner/host/service, the agent, or an `assignment.read` holder); `acknowledge` is the agent's own (`continue | replanned | stopping`, one meeting or all). A deferral (`defers` in a decision, or `deferReturn` by whoever may pause the task) keeps the attendance pause until its time and then the sweep returns it; it moves no assignment.
- **Admission near a meeting.** `admission(agent, expectedMinutes, yieldsAtBoundary?, safeLongRunning?)` is an advisory, read-only answer for the dispatcher (owner/host/service or the agent itself): it looks at the planned live meetings (M01's open lists, at most 1000) the agent will really attend (reachable now, or paused with the owner's invitation) and, if the work would still run when the meeting starts, answers `defer` until the meeting's end unless the work reaches a safe boundary on request or is a safe long-running operation (`start`, with `continuesDuringMeeting`). Work that ends before the meeting, and agents that will not attend, start. It sees occurrences, which M01 makes at the start of each schedule's preparation lead, so the lead is how far ahead it looks.
- **Limits.** (1) Nothing calls `admission` yet: the dispatcher (H12) must ask before it asks H03 for a place, pass `revalidation(taskId)` into its start checks, and compose `PauseService.availability`; the same goes for the clock loop that would call `sweepReturns` and for an invitation/wake/notice transport. (2) A revalidation requirement is enforced for an attendee by its held pause; for an absent agent it is a durable requirement and a question to ask, not a gate: until a start check asks `revalidation`, nothing stops the agent. It does not lift on its own when overdue (`overdue` is shown); an owner who wants a paused attendee released uses `PauseService.resume` on its task gate. (3) A second `pause` on a task gate this attendance holds is refused by L01, so a manual pause requested during the meeting is expressed with `keepPaused`, or by resuming and pausing again (a new request, which the return keeps). If a pause is raced by another caller of `prepare` the pause is recorded as not ours, which fails safe (it stays paused after the meeting). (4) An agent joins once; after `leave` it cannot rejoin that meeting. (5) The room's conversation needs every roster agent to hold `history.read` in the project (C01 refuses otherwise). (6) The team roster is the team's direct active members (child teams are not walked). (7) `callNow` needs `meeting.manage`; its invitations are sent only when the caller is owner/host/service. (8) The decisions are strings and task lists, not M02 decision records. (9) An attendee that never reaches its boundary stays `preparing` or `blocked`; nothing times that out except the room's own lease below. (10) Open rooms are listed in one record (200 at most; a further `openRoom` is `unavailable`).
- **Lease on a room.** A room whose meeting is never ended would hold its attendance pauses for ever, so `sweepReturns` (owner/host/service; to be called by the controller's loop beside M01's `tick`) ends every open room whose occurrence is no longer `planned` (held elsewhere, cancelled, missed) or whose end plus M01's 60-minute grace has passed, as a lapsed meeting: everyone leaves, no decisions are applied, the occurrence is left to M01 (it marks a meeting nobody held `missed` on its own sweep), and each attendee is returned under the usual rules. The bounded exemption has the same expiry.
- **What later cards must know.** M05 should open the room (`openRoom`) when it starts a meeting, call `beginDiscussion` and take the roster of record from `status` (`present` participants), use `briefOf` for any answer from an absent agent (and wrap it as not live), and end through `endMeeting` with the decisions M02/M06 recorded. The delivery rule for participants not holding the floor is M05's.

**M04 review round 1 changes.**
- **The exemption covers only what held the agent at grant time.** `pause-exemption` now records `covers` (agent and team gates with the request that paused each). `PauseService#chain` drops only a covered gate whose current request is still that one (and locks it in the caller's commit); any newer or re-placed pause, any team pause placed during the meeting and every installation pause still blocks the meeting task. `PauseService.agentHolds` lists the gates holding an agent. M01 records `ownerInvitedAt` on a direct invitation (each invitation sets it); a gate paused after it is not covered, and the agent shows `unavailable` until the owner invites again. An installation pause is never overridden (decision: bounded attendance covers only agent and team pauses of an agent the owner invited). Seat, availability and covers are decided again inside the join commit, which throws if the participant left, and a join only ever creates the permission (an ended one is not made active again).
- **Notices cannot hold up returns.** `endMeeting` returns attendees first and sends notices last; a failing or missing sink is caught, the agents come back as `unnotified` in the result, are listed (`meeting-notice-pending`, 200) and retried by `sweepReturns` (which reports `unnotified`). If the list is full the requirement itself (`notifiedAt` absent) is the record.
- **Smaller.** `join` needs a `prepare` (an empty list is fine); `callNow` validates its command ID first; `prepare` recognises its own pause after a crash by the request's operation ID and actor (the same caller normally just replays); the pending-return capacity is checked before a deferred or revalidating outcome is written; `#end` tolerates a missing occurrence; `refresh` updates a changed reason.
- **Lead decisions recorded.** (1) A manual pause requested during a meeting on an interrupted task stays `keepPaused` (L01 still refuses a second pause on a held gate; recording it as a second request on the gate is a change to L01 and is not done here). (2) An attendee's revalidation hold stays until acknowledged; it shows `overdue` after its deadline for the supervisor tier to act on; absent agents get the durable requirement (asymmetry accepted until H12). (3) A manager-called immediate meeting sends invitations only when an owner, host or service dispatches them. (4) The team roster is direct members only. (5) Known gap: a join that fails after its meeting task was created (for example the agent became unavailable meanwhile) leaves that task `in_progress`; a later join reuses it.

## U12 notes: agent messages, delivery classes and the interruption budget (2026-10-23)

`packages/controller/src/messaging/` (`AgentMessageService`), `messages.test.ts` (15 tests on each of the four backends). It sits beside the conversation service (U02), not on top of it: U02 is thread-based and is the way external clients talk to agents; this is the per-agent inbox of task-bound envelopes. New actions `message.send` and `message.urgent` (per project, resource-narrowable); new ID prefix `amg`.

- **Envelope and states.** A message (`agent-message`) carries task, project, sender (the authenticated principal, never a field of the command), recipient, purpose, text, delivery class, urgency, correlation ID (its own ID unless given; a reply inherits its parent's), optional deadline with `escalateTo`, reply link and depth, and the state `accepted -> delivered -> handled -> answered` with a time for each. There is no stored `sending` state: accepting is one atomic step, so a sender whose call did not return repeats it with the same command ID and gets the recorded result (a changed repeat is `command_conflict`). `handled` is the recipient's word. A reply (`replyTo`) takes task, recipient and correlation from its parent, is allowed only to the parent's recipient and only after delivery, and marks the parent `answered` in the same commit. Text is untrusted (`trust: "untrusted-content"`) and credential-shaped text is refused.
- **Classes (README 485-491).** `informational`: read at a check-in or a stand-up (the stand-up names a live meeting occurrence from M01 whose roster the reader is on). `advice`: read at the next safe boundary. `blocking-dependency`: wakes the recipient's task by answering its open `dependency` wait; eligible only if the wait names the sender as resolver, the task is assigned to the recipient and the wait is still open; otherwise the message is stored as advice with `downgraded: "no_eligible_wait"` (visible to the sender in the result). `urgent-control`: an interruption. Which class a boundary may deliver is enforced twice, when reading (`readable`) and when confirming (`acknowledge`), so a recipient cannot pull an informational message out at a safe boundary by ID. Reading never changes anything; `acknowledge` records `delivered` (batch of 50, repeat-safe). Urgency (`low/normal/high/urgent`) is derived from the class and the sender's authority and never chosen separately, so a parent's message is not urgent because of rank; it maps onto U11b's levels later (nothing of U11b is built here).
- **Urgent control.** Needs the sender's own `message.urgent` grant on the task (the owner has it). A manager grant (`agent.manage`), a supervisor or parent team gives nothing (tested with the recipient's supervisor). The recipient has an interruption allowance: 3 per rolling hour from anyone but the owner (`allowance` option, `MESSAGE_LIMITS`); the next one is stored as advice with `downgraded: "interruption_allowance"`. It is delivered only through the `interrupt` boundary, and only while the recipient is available and the task is not paused. The owner is never interrupted or woken by an agent: urgent and blocking classes to an owner are refused.
- **Never wakes a paused agent.** `availability` (production: `PauseService.availability`) and `taskPaused` (`PauseService.isPaused`) are required options and fail closed (a throwing hook means unavailable). A message to a paused or suspended recipient is accepted and reported `queued`, to a stopped one `unavailable`; nothing in the service writes a pause gate (tested by the gate revision not moving), so a message or an inquiry cannot lift a pause. A blocking wake and an interruption are held while the agent, its team chain, the installation or the task is paused, suspended or stopped; a paused agent may still read informational and advice (no resume). `release(recipient)` (owner/host/service) is the pass the controller's loop runs after a resume: it retries held wakes, re-checks the sender's `message.send` grant at that moment, and turns a message whose wait has gone into advice. A wake that died between answering the wait and recording delivery is finished by the repeat (the wait's answer text and answerer identify it). `inquiryReach` reports `queued/unavailable/ready` for an H04 inquiry's addressee; asking never wakes anyone.
- **Focus periods and coalescing.** `setFocus` (the agent itself, the owner or an `agent.manage` holder; at most 4 hours ahead): during it `safe-boundary` returns and delivers nothing (reported as withheld by focus); check-ins, stand-ups, blocking wakes and authorized interruptions are not affected. A message with a `coalesceKey` replaces the text of the one waiting message with the same sender, task, class and key (count kept, queue unchanged); informational and advice only, never with a deadline or as a reply.
- **Limits (provisional, `MESSAGE_LIMITS`).** 30 messages a minute per sender and 10 a minute per sender and recipient; 100 waiting messages per recipient and 20 per sender at one recipient (`unavailable` with the reason); reply chains 8 deep; text 4000, purpose 200. The queue lives in one inbox record per recipient (at most 100 for peers, 20 more only for authorized interruptions, 20 more only for escalations, and 20 more only for the owner: hard bound 160), so listing it is not a truncated scan.
- **Deadlines and escalation.** A request with a deadline must name `escalateTo` (an agent or the owner, reachable for the task); deadlines are at most 30 days out, 20 open per sender, 1000 in all (one index record, so no scan is truncated). `settleDue` (owner/host/service; the controller's loop must call it, it uses the injected clock and no storage timers, which are shared with S08) writes one `escalation` message from `controller` (advice class, correlation of the request) to the named path for each unanswered request past its deadline, once; a reply removes the request from the index. A full escalation path leaves the request for the next pass (`blocked`). Nobody is interrupted by an escalation.
- **Questions to the owner.** `askOwner` is H04's inquiry addressed to the installation owner (the asker needs `attempt.work` on the task; `waitId` links an `inquiry` wait whose resolver is the owner) plus a controller-side `owner-question` notification (U11) when a notification service is supplied. `ownerQuestions(owner)` is what the owner chat shows: question, asker, task title and state, project name, and the linked wait (summary, next action, deadline). `answerOwner` answers the inquiry, which answers the wait and makes the task ready; repeating it finishes missing steps.
- **What it does not do.** No wiring: nothing calls `release`, `settleDue` or the boundary reads yet (the dispatcher/H12 and the check-in loop must; `start()` does not build the service); no state-API route or chat command (`ownerQuestions` is the read the chat should use); delivered messages are read by ID only (no history listing); a coalesced message keeps its first `sentAt`; a task-level pause is consulted for wakes and interruptions but a stand-up read is not blocked by it; escalation text is built from the request's purpose and ids (the purpose is the sender's, bounded and credential-checked). Rate windows are fixed one-minute windows, not sliding. Allowance counts accepted urgent requests, including ones queued for a paused recipient.
- **For later cards.** U11b should map `urgency` and the `downgraded` reasons onto Notice / Needs you / Urgent; the dispatcher should deliver `readable(..., "interrupt")` results to the runtime as a safe-boundary interruption (L05 `interrupt`) and call `release` after every resume.

**U12 review round 1 changes.**
- **The owner is not locked out.** The 100-message queue is for everyone, but authorized interruptions (the sender holds `message.urgent` and the allowance has room) may use a 20-place reserve, escalations a further 20, and the owner a final 20 that nobody else can use; so a queue holds at most 160 and the owner cannot be locked out by peers, by slow interruptions or by escalations (tested with all three reserves full). The owner is exempt from the per-minute, per-recipient and per-sender-queue limits and has a 50-place reserve on the 1000 open deadlines; an authorized interruption is exempt from the per-sender queue limit (the allowance bounds it). A peer without the grant is still refused when the queue is full. The owner's 50-place reserve on the open-deadline cap is tested with a small cap (`deadlineCap` and `ownerDeadlineReserve` options).
- **Rolling allowance.** The inbox keeps the times of the interruptions accepted in the last window (at most 3) and drops older ones, so there is no fixed window to reset (tested with the 0, 58, 59, 61, 62, 63 pattern).
- **Injected clock for grants.** The wake's re-check of the sender and the recipient/escalation reach check now pass the controller's clock to `allows` (tested with expiring grants made through `GrantService`).
- **A wake commits with the pause gates.** `WaitService.answerWait` takes an optional `{ beforeCommit }` (changes merged with `mergeChanges`, may refuse by throwing); the wake passes `PauseService.guard(taskId)` (a required `guard` option), so the gates of the task, its assignee agent, the agent's teams and the installation commit with the answer, and a pause landing in between makes the commit fail and the wake decide again (tested with a hook that pauses between the check and the commit, for an agent pause and a task pause; team and installation pauses are in the wake-holds test). A suspension or stop (`otherAvailability`) is not a gate and cannot be locked this way; it is checked just before. This is the one change outside the package (`domain/waits.ts`, covered by the messaging tests; the existing wait tests pass).
- **Wake answer.** The text written into the wait is `[<message ID>] <purpose>: <text>`; a blocking dependency that would not fit in the wait's answer limit is refused at send (never cut). The crash-repair path now recognises its own answer by that ID, so an identical text sent twice is not reported as a wake for the second message (tested).
- **Escalation.** It is stored with the target's real reach; at the deadline the target must still be active and able to read the task, otherwise the escalation goes to the owner with a note (it is never lost).
- **Lead decisions.** (1) `escalateTo` goes to the supervisor tier first: a sender that has a supervisor must name one of its supervisors; the owner (or anyone else) may be named only by a sender with no supervisor path. (2) No stored `sending` state (atomic accept, idempotent by command ID). (3) Downgrading to advice instead of refusing is preferred, and the sender is told. (4) `answerOwner` now closes the owner-question notification; the owner chat/state-API route and the dispatcher wiring (H12) are follow-ups for U11/U11b. (5) Provisional limits stay.
## C06b notes: cache-stable Pi prompts across context switches, and stored thinking (2026-10-23)

Fixed on the NimbleNewt side; **no Pi patch** (the measured gap is in how callers assemble sections and tools and in the cache key, not in Pi's code). New: `packages/pi-adapter/src/prompt-layout.ts` (layout, sorted tools, stability check, volatile wrapper), `cache-shaping.ts` (`shapeModels`, `PrefixTracker`, split of the shared system block), `thinking.ts`; `packages/controller/src/inference-cache/shaping.ts` (`piCachePolicy`, `sharedPrefixBreakpoints`) and `simulators.ts` (the C06 test servers, moved out of `adapter.test.ts` and exported through `@nimblenewt/controller/testing`, plus a `timings` response shape for Radiance and Strata); `packages/controller/src/context/thinking.ts` (`ThinkingService`); `AuditEvent` gains `prompt_layout_change` and `thinking_dropped`, contracts gain `StoredThinking` and `ThinkingReader`. Tests: `prompt-cache.test.ts` 10, `thinking.test.ts` 4 (pi-adapter); `thinking.test.ts` 5 on each of four storage backends plus 1 (controller); `shaping.test.ts` 4 (controller); `c06b-cache-stability.test.ts` 6, one per backend (integration). Small edits to C06: the Claude profile takes a new breakpoint `{ systemBlock: n }`, and the llama.cpp/vLLM simulators now count tools before messages (C06's 19 adapter tests still pass).

**Audit of Pi Durable 1.3.0 and pi-ai 1.3.0 (what is measured, with the bytes pi-ai builds for the provider)**
- Pi renders no system text of its own. Each request it renders the agent's sections (extension sections in install order, then `instructions`) and the offered tools (registry order) and writes `pi.system` entries: a complete baseline first, later only patches (a changed section value, `null` to delete, tools added or removed). If a patch would change the order it writes remove-all then re-add-all. Tool order is whatever the registry holds, so two agents declaring the same tools in different orders have different prompts.
- Before the request Pi moves the first system message to the front; any later `pi.system` entry stays where it was appended, so Pi's own log only ever grows. **What the provider sees is decided by pi-ai**: unless the model's `compat.supportsMidConvoSystemMessages` is true (off for most), pi-ai folds every system message into the one at the head, so a section or tool change rewrites the head and loses every cached prefix after it (the card's description is right for default models; on a model that takes mid-conversation system messages the change is appended and the prefix survives; both tested). Message and system `timestamp` fields are not on the wire.
- Anthropic: breakpoints on the last system block, the last tool and the last user message (confirmed in the built body); render order tools, system, messages. A string user message becomes a one-block array when it is the last one and a plain string again on the next turn: the content is equal, the representation differs (**unverified whether Anthropic's prefix hash treats them as the same; G10 to confirm**; the simulator treats them as equal). A thinking block without a signature is replayed as plain text.
- OpenAI-style: `prompt_cache_key` comes from Pi's provider session ID, a random UUID per conversation (`pi.provider`), and **a fork gets a fresh one**: two agents, a parent and its inquiry branch, never share a key.
- Suspend and resume (restart on the same storage): the replayed head and history are the same bytes (tested over a JSONL store with the tools passed in another order and the layout rebuilt). Inquiry branch: the same head and the parent's request is a prefix of the branch's. Compaction: the head stays (Pi writes a new baseline with identical content), the messages are replaced by the summary, so sharing ends at the first replaced message; the cost is reported per request as `PrefixReport` (`sharedChars` of `chars`, `firstChanged`).

**What NimbleNewt now does**
- **Stable-first layout** (`PiAdapterOptions.promptLayout`): tools sorted by name (always, even without a layout; `AdapterTool.shared` tools first so agents with extra tools still share what follows), then sections `nn_preamble` (common to every agent), `nn_project`, `nn_agent`, then nothing volatile: per-turn content is sent as a user message (`volatileContext`). `assertStableText` refuses dates (including ISO date-times), HH:MM and HH:MM:SS times, UUIDs, 10 to 13 digit epoch numbers, generated IDs and long hex strings in any section. It folds the text first (NFKC, so fullwidth digits count; invisible format characters such as a zero-width space removed; every dash a hyphen) and matches digits with lookarounds, not `\b`, so `seed_1792741784932` and `2026-10-23T07:49:44.932Z` are caught. It is a blunt guard on text we author, not a proof; a section must not hold anything that varies. The text is fixed at open and changes only through `PiAdapter.changePromptLayout(next, reason)`, audited with fingerprints (never text); a test shows the change rewrites the head (`firstChanged 0`).
- **Cache key per shared prefix, not per session**: `PiAdapterOptions.cache.policy(model)` returns `{ key, retention, payload }`; `shapeModels` replaces Pi's session ID with the key, sets `cacheRetention`, and runs `payload` on the real provider body through `onPayload`. The controller side is `piCachePolicy(profile, context, { splitSystem })`: the key is `context.shareKey` (a project or meeting, derived by C06's `deriveKey`) else the scope key; `payload` is the C06 profile's own `prepare` (slot, `cache_salt`, `prompt_cache_key`, breakpoints), so each backend's tuning has one home; retention is sent only where a request can set it (Claude, OpenAI with extended retention); a generic profile yields nothing.
- **Claude**: the Pi adapter sends the shared system text as a block of its own and `piCachePolicy` places the four breakpoints after the tools, after that block, after the agent's text and on the last message (hour for the three head ones with `retention: "long"`, five minutes for the tail). This closes C06 decision (2): Pi's own markers are replaced by the profile's, and the profile is the single owner of marker placement when a policy is given.
- **Compaction** is bounded by C07b (decline on timeout or failure, never Pi's own summariser); this card adds the report: every request carries `PrefixReport`, so the cost of a compaction (how much of the next request was shared with the last) is a number.
- **Measured per backend** (`c06b-cache-stability.test.ts`: two agents of one project alternate three rounds; every request is the exact pi-ai body sent to a simulated server of that backend and read with that backend's profile and hit fields; compared with the layout a naive caller gets from Pi: role first, a clock section, tools in each agent's own order, Pi's per-conversation key). Tokens computed over six requests, NimbleNewt against naive: llama.cpp 3037 vs 3119 (a slot per agent; nothing is shared across slots); vLLM, Radiance, Strata, OpenAI 1719 vs 3139 (81% against 64% served from cache); Claude 2322 vs 11835 (81% against 0%: the naive clock section changes the system block every turn). The second agent's first request reads the shared head on every backend that shares by position. **The servers are simulators of position-based reuse, with response shapes from source or documentation; the figures are for comparison, not measurements of real servers. G10 must run this against real backends and record them.**

**Stored thinking (owner decision 2026-10-22: stored and replayed on a context switch, deleted 14 days after the task, chats keep only words said)**
- **Stored and replayed by Pi, shown by us.** Pi keeps the provider's thinking blocks (Anthropic signed and redacted thinking, OpenAI reasoning items) in the assistant entry and replays them, in order, to the model that produced them. Tested: the blocks reach the provider unchanged on the next turn, after a restart, and after the harness changed (other tools, layout, settings); the built Anthropic body carries `thinking` with its signature and `redacted_thinking` with its data. `AdapterConversation.thinking()` gives the owner's view (readable text, `opaque` for redacted blocks, provider and model, entry); it never carries the signature or encrypted payload. `transcript()` (words only) and `ConversationPort` do not carry thinking; `ThinkingReader` is a separate interface, so code handed a conversation port cannot reach it by type.
- **Model change**: the new model gets only the blocks it can accept (pi-ai's rule: same provider, API and model; the others are left out of the request, nothing is deleted). `shapeModels` reports it once per change (`onThinkingDropped`, audit `thinking_dropped` with the model and the counts); switching back replays the first model's own blocks again (tested both ways). A block with nothing in it is neither kept nor dropped.
- **`ThinkingService`** (controller): `read` is the owner's, paged by an (entry, block) cursor (`next`; one entry can hold several blocks), with a reason (credential-shaped reasons refused), follows the A10 inspection policy (`denied` refuses), redacts the text with `redactText` exactly as private scratch, and records a look (who, task, why, the agent whose thinking it was, the policy, how many, when; never the text) in the same step, the policy check included, so a completed read still replays after the policy turns to `denied`; the owner and the agent whose thinking it was see the looks (as A10 requires for scratch inspections), no other agent does; a repeat of the command returns the look and no text. **Everyone who is not the owner (agent, service, host, a task-scoped principal) gets the same `not_found` as a task with no thinking, before the source is even asked.** `forExport` returns nothing unless the owner has turned `setExportPolicy` on (default off), then redacted text only, never opaque blocks, and only for owner, host or service; `recordDrop` (owner, host, service) and `drops` keep the model-change record; `deleteAfter(endedAt)` is the 14-day date for K06 (`THINKING_RETENTION_DAYS`). The source of the blocks (`ThinkingSource`) is given to the service at construction, never by a caller.

**Limits and what later cards must know**
1. The dispatcher that builds `cache.policy` per route from the endpoint config and hands it to `PiAdapter.open` does not exist yet; the integration test is that glue. A model change of one adapter needs the policy function to look at the model it is given (it does).
2. One layout per adapter: agent-specific instructions are per adapter, not per task conversation of one adapter. Agents that share a prefix on Claude need the same tool set (restrict by policy, not by declaration); on other backends differing extra tools are fine if the common ones are `shared`.
3. A provider rejection of thinking for the same model (a signature that no longer verifies) is not retried here; only the model-change drop is handled. It surfaces as a provider error for R02.
4. The adapter has no principals: reading `thinking()` is open to whoever holds the concrete `AdapterConversation`; the owner-only rule is the controller's (`ThinkingService`), so the wiring must give `ThinkingSource` only to it. No export feature exists yet; `forExport` is what it must call. U09 shows the owner's view, K06 deletes by `deleteAfter`.
5. Cache fallback after a refused setting (C06's `CacheAdapter.call`) does not apply to Pi's own sends; Pi's retry and R02 handle those errors.
6. The `PrefixTracker` compares by hash on Pi's context (with the wire collapse applied), not on the provider body; the provider-body check is the test's. It remembers 8 requests per key and 256 keys, hashes only.
7. `shapeModels` wraps `streamSimple` only for the conversation's own requests. Pi's compaction summary is a `completeSimple` call; the wrapper sends it with `cacheRetention: "none"` and no shared key (a summary is not a prefix of anything). `streamDeferred` only fetches an answer already requested and is not shaped. An observer that throws is reported through `onReport` and never changes the request.
9. **Isolation.** For a project with `isolationRequired` on a backend whose isolation is `none`, `piCachePolicy` returns no key and retention `none`, runs the profile's plain preparation and deletes `prompt_cache_key` and `prompt_cache_retention` from the body (tested for every profile; vLLM's salt isolates, so its cache stays on).
10. **Slotted servers (llama.cpp with slots).** C06's slot partitioning (one turn per slot, holder check and clear, holder update) wraps the HTTP call, which Pi makes itself. Until the dispatcher sends Pi's calls through `CacheAdapter`, `piCachePolicy` names no slot and sets reuse off for a slotted profile (`degraded` says so), so Pi's own calls can never read another project's KV; the price is no slot reuse through Pi. **Dispatcher requirement:** route Pi's requests through `CacheAdapter` with the route's shared `EndpointState`, then lift this. Strata's pin is only sent when the context says `pinAuthorized`, which must come from a resolved pin marker.
11. **Accepted by the lead:** agents that share a Claude prefix use the same tool set and are restricted by policy at call time (a tool may be declared to an agent that cannot use it; the denial says why); tools are always sorted, so the first requests after the upgrade start one cold cache.
8. G10: capture real responses and Anthropic's treatment of the string/array user content (see the audit), then replace the simulator figures above.

## U04 notes: conversational policy editing (2026-10-22)

`packages/controller/src/chat/` gains `PolicyEditService` (`policy-edit.ts`), the closed vocabulary of policy changes (`policy-intent.ts`) and a new intent, `policy_edit`, in the chat interpreter; `PolicyService.contextFor` (A07) gives an agent the policy as it binds it. One test file, `policy-edit.test.ts` (3 unit tests and 24 tests on each of the four backends; mutation-checked, see the list at the end). U03's `Intent` gained `policy_edit` (and `lengthDays` on `apply_proposal`) and the interpreter is given `now`; the chat plan has a `policy` item, and `apply_proposal`/`discard_proposal` accept an edit ID (`pedit-...`).

- **What an edit is.** One `policy-edit` record per instruction: the owner's wording as said (their own text, never quoted material), the evidence phrase, the change as the interpreter stated it, what it was understood as in plain lines (built from the typed operation, never from model text), what it means in practice and what stays unchanged, the conditions that were not applied, the direction (`tighten`, `loosen`, `neutral`, `unknown`), the state (`proposed`, `applying`, `applied`, `noop`, `refused`, `stale`, `discarded`), the authenticated origin and a forwarding agent apart, the revisions it produced (`applied`) and when they take effect. A refused edit is recorded too, so the wording is not lost. Open edits are listed in a per-owner index record (at most 50 open; no scan can run short) and the interpreter is shown them, so "yes, that one" can name one.
- **Same door.** Nothing is a second way to change a policy. An edit ends in a call to the service the API uses, under the owner's own principal: `PolicyService.set` (profile, cells, locks, the all-command gate, a project's environment), `ReviewService.setPolicy` (reserve a tool for the owner), `GrantService.grant`/`revoke` (approval grants, `approve.class.<class>` or `approve.tool.<tool>`, never a capability grant), `ConfigurationService.stage`/`apply` (settings of the U10 layers; a failed check discards the draft and nothing is in force). Cells are named by the A07 action classes and tiers (development and testing are one tier, `free`; the reply says so). The owner only: `apply`, `create`, `prepare`, `open` and `discard` refuse any other principal (a task-scoped owner credential too).
- **What applies at once and what is shown first.** An edit that only makes things stricter or changes nothing about the gates (a cell asked more, a lock, the all-command gate on, a reserved tool, a revoked grant, a project moved to a stricter or equal environment) applies in the message when a non-question sentence of the owner's own text holds the evidence phrase and an order verb (`ownerOrders`, fixed list) and the message carried no quoted material. Everything that asks less or gives more (a cell asked less, a profile that asks less, a grant, an unlock, the gate off, a freer environment) and every configuration value is saved as a proposal with the preview and applies only on a later explicit request (`apply_proposal` naming the edit, or `PolicyEditService.apply`); never in the message that drew it up (tested). This follows U03 (authority-bearing fields are proposals in every profile; explicit orders need a verb) and the guiding principle for the safe direction.
- **Scope preserved.** A cell edit changes exactly one cell in exactly one scope (installation or one project); the preview lists every cell whose mode changes and the reply says "every other class, environment and project is unchanged". Locks, other cells, reserved tools and the profile are untouched (tested with a locked cell, a reserved tool, another project and the installation table).
