A project edit looser than a locked installation cell is refused with the lock named (the policy service would refuse it too). A profile switch keeps the cells, locks and reserved tools (tested).
- **Mandatory human gates are never weakened from chat.** A tool the project reserves for the owner can be added (stricter) but never released by chat (`mandatory_gate`, recorded); a grant for a reserved tool, for access changes, for an unclassified tool, without audiences for sends and publication, or without a spend limit for spending is refused; loosening the cell of a class does not release a reserved tool (tested through the policy engine). A standing grant is always `source: "standing"`, so it can never waive what stays the owner's decision, and the reply says so.
- **Unsupported conditions are explicitly unapplied.** The interpreter lists wishes the vocabulary cannot say as `conditions`. Each is reported as not applied, in the reply and in the record (a condition that is not in the owner's words is replaced by a generic phrase, so it cannot smuggle text in). An edit that loosens anything and has such a condition is not applied at all (`unsupported_condition`, nothing changed), because dropping the condition would make it wider than asked; a stricter edit applies without it and says so. Time limits on a cell are not supported (a cell has no expiry); "until Friday" is expressed as a grant with a date.
- **Concurrent edits.** Each edit pins the revision of the record it will write when it is drawn up (installation or project policy, review policy, configuration layer) and passes it as the expected revision, so an edit made meanwhile through the API or the screen is detected by the service itself: the edit becomes `stale` (stated in plain words, nothing overwritten, the other edit stands) and cannot be applied again; the owner says it again to see it against the policy as it is now (tested with a proposal and with an edit that applies at once and loses the race).
- **Crash and repeat.** Applying has three steps: the time and the length of a grant are fixed and stored (`applying`), the service call runs under a command ID derived from the edit (a retry replays it), then the revisions are recorded (`applied`). A second request, a concurrent one and a retry after a crash do the same thing once; a grant retried days later keeps the expiry decided at the first try (tested with a controlled clock).
- **Standing grants (owner decision on length).** A proposed grant shows the length options (1, 7, 30 and 90 days, `GRANT_LENGTH_OPTIONS`) and the length the system would choose with the reason in plain words. A length the owner stated in words (`lengthDays`, or `until` a date, used exactly) is used as stated and is offered beside the fixed options; the owner picks one at apply time (`lengthDays` of the apply request or of the `apply_proposal` intent; one that was not offered is refused). When nothing is stated the fallback is 30 days (`chooseGrantLength`) with the explanation "no pattern of earlier approvals to go on"; the explanation is kept in the edit and in the grant's `basis`. A grant is at most 365 days, expires by itself and is revocable. Without an environment named the grant covers development and testing only. **Not done here:** deriving the default from earlier approvals, a one-off marking and the cap at the longest option (card A07d, still planned); `chooseGrantLength` is where its inputs go. The grant's explanation is not yet recorded through C09.
- **Forwarded messages.** `PolicyEditService.forward(agent, { threadId, seq })` reads the message from the conversation record (so the agent must be able to read the thread; otherwise it is missing), takes the origin from the stored, authenticated sender (a message from anyone but the owner is refused: "an arbitrary message is not an owner instruction"; a claimed sender counts for nothing; a message altered by credential redaction is refused), asks the same interpreter, and records each instruction as an edit with `origin` (the owner, `via: "conversation"`, thread and message) and `forwardedBy` (the agent) apart. A forwarded edit is only ever proposed, even if it is stricter, and only the owner applies it; the agent gets edit IDs and states, not the preview. Forwarding the same message again names the same edits.
- **Next agent context.** `PolicyService.contextFor(principal, projectId)` reads the records at the moment of the call (nothing cached): profile, gate, environment, which classes run, go to review or ask the owner in the project's environment, the tools reserved for the owner, the approvals the principal holds, the record revisions and the latest changes with who made them. A change through the API, the screen or a conversation appears in the next call (tested); a principal asks about itself only and an inactive one gets nothing. The agent-context builder (H12) must call it.
- **Decisions I made.** Policy edits are owner-only in chat; a loosening or configuration edit is always a proposal (the plan text of A07 and U03 say authority is shown first; the guiding principle is kept for the safe direction); a refused or no-change edit is recorded; evidence for a policy edit must be a phrase in the owner's own text like any other chat command; standing grants made from chat are `standing`, project-specific (no `*`), development and testing by default; a project edit never makes a cell looser than a locked installation cell; the gate cannot be set off at a project while another level keeps it on (reported as no change); setting edits always ask first because their effect on authority cannot be judged without the definition's meaning.
- **Limits stated.** The interpreter is the model call: it can state a wish wrongly, but cannot add words, tools or values the owner did not say (checked), cannot pass quoted text (refused), and every edit is checked against records, policy and revisions; an interpreter that leaves a condition out of `conditions` makes the edit wider than the owner meant, which for a loosening the owner sees in the preview before applying. No restriction (`GrantService.restrict`) or review-policy edit other than reserving a tool can be made by chat yet; settings of the team, agent and task layers need the ID of the team, agent or task (no names); a value is checked by the configuration service when applied, not when previewed (a refusal at that point is reported in plain words and leaves nothing staged); previews of settings show the current value for the installation layer only; time zones are the interpreter's job (`now` is given to it, dates are UTC). Policy edits are not part of a coordinator proposal. The chat reply is plain lines; screens, transport and the digest are other cards. A forwarded edit's list in the owner's views (U08/U11) is not built.
- **Mutation checks (all killed by a test).** Loosening applies at once; unsupported condition dropped on a loosening; the revision shown replaced by one read at apply time; forward accepts any sender; a forwarded edit may apply at once; unreserve goes through; a grant for a reserved tool; any sentence counts as an order; quoted text does not make a proposal; apply by the message that drew the edit up (also the discard); any grant length accepted; a grant made `direct`; the grant's time and length fixed again on a retry; a failed setting left staged; no-change not detected; the open index not cleared; a locked installation cell not checked; tool text not compared with the owner's words; a grant for access changes; a send grant without audience; the context without project changes; the context for an inactive principal; edit apply not routed by chat; the origin recorded as the forwarder; a stale edit reported as a generic refusal. One mutation (the expected revision left out of the policy call) survives by design: the policy service itself refuses a write to an existing record without one.

**U04 review follow-up (2026-10-23).** (1) An at-once edit needs an order of its own move: one verb list per move (revoke, lock, reserve, gate on, cell, profile, environment; `ownerOrders(evidence, wording, move)`), and a sentence with a negation (don't, do not, never, not, no, keep, leave, without) or a musing (maybe, should we, I wonder, what if, let me think) is never an order, so "Keep ann's approval", "Don't revoke it", "Never switch to strict" and a musing about the gate are proposals in every profile (tested in strict, derived and autonomous). A proposal is the safe fallback: "Never push without asking me" is therefore shown first. (2) A project's environment is judged by what would be decided: each class's effective mode (overrides and locks included) in the old and the new tier, plus every active approval grant of the project that names the new environment and not the old (a bounded scan; an unfinished scan counts as loosening); any loosening is a proposal and the changes are listed in the preview. (3) A move to a freer profile (strict, derived, autonomous) is a loosening whatever the cell preview shows. (4) The grant length on apply is taken from `apply_proposal` only when the number (or "a week", "a month"...) is in the owner's own words; otherwise the length shown is used. (5) `PolicyService.contextFor` returns nothing unless the project exists and the principal holds a grant on it (or is the owner); a nonexistent project and a project without a grant read alike. Also: discarding an edit by chat without the service is refused as unavailable; edits an agent passed on have their own index and limit (50) and are marked "(passed on by agent:x)" for the interpreter and in the reply; an edit stuck in `applying` can be discarded 15 minutes (injected clock) after it started and the revisions recorded so far are listed (steps of a multi-step edit are noted as they complete; a step in flight may be missing); the revision of a reserved-tool write is derived from the expected one, not read again; a grant's environments and spend limit must be in the owner's words like its tool. Lead decisions (the owner will be asked later): loosening edits and configuration settings stay proposals in every profile because they widen authority (a clear owner order still needs the explicit apply; revisit once the standing-rule machinery A07b/A07d can propose it); chat-made grants stay standing, one project, development and testing by default; chat never releases a reserved tool; refused and no-change edits stay recorded. Mutation checks added: negation, musing, one verb list for all moves, freer profile not a loosening, grants and effective modes ignored for environments, no grant check or project check in the context, interpreter-supplied length, silent discard without the service, no lease, forwarded edits sharing the owner's index (all killed).

## M02 notes: votes, decisions and meeting summaries (2026-10-23)

`packages/controller/src/meetings/` gained `record.ts` (`MeetingRecordService`, `taskUpdateApplier`), `record-types.ts`, `votes.ts` (the pure counting rules), `summary.ts` (the pure summary builder) and two test files: `record.test.ts` (23 tests on each of the four backends, 92 runs) and `votes.test.ts` (6 pure tests). New ID prefixes `mrc` (meeting record), `mmo` (motion), `mbl` (ballot), `mdn` (decision), `msu` (summary); entries are `mte-<record>-<seq>`. No existing service was changed apart from `domain/ids.ts` (the prefixes). Mutation-checked: 41 deliberate breaks of the rules were each caught by at least one test (list at the end). Scope: the *records and rules* of a meeting; the meeting procedure (floor, rounds, chair's questions, objection check, owner inquiry period, the chair's read of an absent member's desk) is M05/M06 and calls these commands.

- **A transcript of typed entries.** One `meeting-record` per live occurrence (`open`: owner, host, service or `meeting.manage`; the occurrence must be a planned live meeting; the roster is the occurrence's, copied once, and the members M01 withheld (paused, suspended, stopped) are *absent*, not abstaining). Entries are `statement`, `owner-message`, `motion`, `amendment`, `ruling`, `vote`, `correction` and `presence`, appended one record each under a rising sequence number (the record's head is updated in the same commit, so numbers are unique and gap-free; tested with six parallel writers). Motions, amendments and votes come only from their own commands; `append` takes statements, owner messages (owner only), rulings (the current chair; the owner can overrule one) and corrections. **Nothing is edited:** a correction is a new entry that names the one it corrects; the discussion view returns the original `text` and, beside it, `currentText` and `correctedBy`. A statement may be tagged `update`, `question`, `action` or `plan` (with an assignee and task); tagged items (100 at most) live on the record so the summary needs no scan. An agent writes only as itself; the controller (owner, host, service) may write down a roster agent's turn with `speaker`, and the entry then shows both `by` and `speaker`. No operation accepts an actor.
- **Motions and ballots.** A motion has versions; every ballot is bound to one version. A *material* amendment (chair, owner, host or service) makes a new version, sets the vote back to `proposed` and needs a fresh vote; a non-material wording change is allowed only before voting opens. A ballot (a roster member who can be asked, as itself; a reason is required, abstentions too) is one aggregate per motion and voter, so a second session of the same agent is the same voter. It is hidden while the vote is open (the owner and the voter see it; everyone else is told "hidden") and revealed together with the tally in the closing `vote` entry and the decision record. A voter may **revise** only because of an owner message that came after its ballot, once per message (`ownerMessageEntryId`); each ballot and revision stays in the ballot's history with its trigger; the last one counts. The owner (or host/service) can **hold** an open vote (no ballot cast or revised, its clock stops and is given back on resume). Every ballot also bumps the motion's revision, so a close, an amendment or a hold at the same moment either sees the ballot or makes it retry: no acknowledged ballot can be missing from a tally. One vote at a time per meeting.
- **Counting (owner decision 2026-10-22).** Quorum is a majority of the whole fixed roster (`floor(n/2)+1`), counted in ballots cast, abstentions included; absent members count as nothing and silence is never a yes; the owner is not on the roster and never needed. A simple majority of yes against no decides. If too few members can be asked for a binding vote to be possible, `openVote` is refused ("binding vote deferred") and discussion and reports go on; if the time limit passes with too few ballots the motion ends `no-quorum` (nothing decided, nothing applied). **Tie:** a chair on the roster who cast yes or no already has that one ballot, and a tie goes to its side (`chair-ballot`, counted once). A chair off the roster, or on it but abstaining or silent, may cast one casting vote, only on a tie, only the chair the vote opened under, within the vote's time limit again; it is recorded as `casting-vote` in the decision's `tie` and in the vote entry, and is *not* in the yes/no counts. With no chair, a changed chair, an absent chair, no answer in time or nobody voting yes or no, the tie fails (`failed`). While a casting vote is awaited nothing is revealed.
- **Decisions.** A concluded vote makes a `meeting-decision`: outcome, tally, the table of every voter's last ballot and reason, the dissent (ballots against the outcome) and abstentions, a **link to the discussion** (`fromSeq`..`toSeq` and the entry IDs of the motion, its amendments, rulings and the vote: `discussion(record, {fromSeq, toSeq})` returns exactly that), scope, changes and the owner's response. `why(decision)` answers "why was this decided" from the record alone, line by line with entry numbers (who moved it, the amendments and rulings, each member's vote and reason, the result, how a tie was broken, why it is a decision or only a recommendation, the owner's answer, what became of each change).
- **Effective authority; adopted versus applied.** A motion carries proposed *changes* (up to 10): a kind that the controller registered an applier for (`options.appliers`; `taskUpdateApplier(projects)` is provided), a roster agent that carries it, a target in this project, the target's revision it was proposed against, and a patch. An action that is a permission, budget, configuration, routing, catalog, environment, MCP or recovery action is refused (`NEVER`): a motion widens nothing. An adopted decision is **in scope** only if (1) its agenda item (if any) lets the team act (M03's request type: a discussion or a recommendation request is advisory; an explicit owner instruction is not put to a vote at all), (2) no owner decision (C08) stands or is disputed on its topic, and (3) at least one carrying agent holds the action *now* (A02 grants and restrictions, read through `options.authority` at the moment, never from a caller). Otherwise it is a **recommendation** and every change is recorded `not-applied`. `apply` (owner, host, service) carries out the changes one at a time and judges each just before it acts: the owner's response, an owner `implementation-hold` or a superseded decision on a task (C08's `guard`), the carrier's authority now (a grant revoked or a restriction added since the meeting is honoured as gone, and nothing here grants or reopens anything), that the target still exists and **is still at the proposed revision** (otherwise `not-applied`, code `conflict`, with the revision it is at). Progress is saved before the effect (`applying`) and after it, and the change's command ID is stable (`mtg:<decision>:<change>`), so a crash and a repeat apply it once (tested). The summary keeps `adopted` (what the meeting voted for) apart from `applied` / `notApplied` and has a `consistent` check that every counted change is in exactly one list.
- **Owner presence.** `ownerJoin` / `ownerLeave` (only the authenticated owner) are durable `presence` entries; the arrival entry carries a report built from the records: decisions adopted so far, changes applied, not applied, not yet applied, motions that did not pass, motions still open, so an outcome already executed is reported accurately. With the owner present when the vote concludes, an adopted in-scope decision with changes is `ownerResponse: pending`: `apply` waits ("silence leaves the decision pending") while unrelated work goes on, and leaving authorizes nothing. The owner answers with `ownerRespond`: `agree` (then applied), `decline` (the changes not yet applied are refused; those already applied stay reported as applied) or `alternative`, which records the owner's own wording as a separate **C08 owner decision** (`DecisionService.record`, not a copy) and links it; no re-vote. The vote and the owner's decision are separate records. With the owner absent, an in-scope decision is actionable at once.
- **Summary.** `summary()` is built from the records alone every time it is read, so it always matches what was applied: attendance (present, absent with M01's reason, roster members who did not take part, the chair, the owner's joins), key updates, decisions with reasons and a discussion link, dissent, unresolved (unanswered questions, motions tabled, void, failed, without quorum or never put to a vote, decisions waiting for the owner), actions, and the plan. `summarize` (owner, host, service) also writes a durable snapshot (and the project's latest stand-up plan); the optional `narrate` hook adds a short overview, and **if it throws or returns junk the snapshot is still written** with `overviewFailed` set. `finish` (owner, host, service; `interrupted` marks a meeting cut short) voids every motion that was not concluded (an open vote included: "not concluded"), keeps the transcript whole and leaves the occurrence for the caller to `settle`.
- **Unverifiable conclusions.** A statement, a motion or a ballot can cite records (`sources`: kind, id, revision, 5 at most). `options.shareable(cite)` says whether a cited record can be shared with the meeting's readers; by default nothing can. A conclusion that rests on a record that cannot be shared is published as a *claim*, `verifiable: false`, and every read strips the citation for anyone but the owner (and marks the object `unverifiable`); `unverifiableCount` is in the summary. `why` and `discussion` grant nothing: no grant is created, the source is not opened, and the citation is not revealed (tested by comparing the readers' grants before and after, and the whole answer for the source ID). The chair's meeting-scoped read of an absent member's desk (M05) is the only exception and is not built here.
- **Stand-up plans persist.** `plan` items from the record (and the summary) are stored in a per-project `meeting-plan` record when the summary is written; `latestPlan(viewer, project)` (owner, `meeting.read`, `meeting.manage`) returns the most recent meeting's plan and survives a restart; an older meeting's refresh does not bring an old plan back.
- **Authorization recap.** `open`, `setChair`, `ownerJoin`..., reads: see above. `apply`, `finish`, `summarize`, `sweep`, `hold`, `resume` are owner, host or service only (a task credential never); `openVote`, `table`, `amend`, `closeVote` are the chair, or owner, host, service; `castingVote` only the chair the vote opened under; `castBallot` only a roster member as itself; `ownerJoin/Leave/Respond` only the owner. Reads of what the viewer may not see look like a missing record (the record, its entries, motions, decisions, ballots, summary and plan; a roster member and the chair read their meeting; the project's readers need `meeting.read` or `meeting.manage`; another project's manager gets nothing). Text is bounded and credential-shaped text is refused.
- **M04 hand-off.** `attendanceDecisions(recordId)` (owner, host, service) turns the adopted, in-scope decisions that change tasks into the `MeetingDecision` list M04's `endMeeting` takes (`ref` = the decision ID, `supersedes` = the tasks changed, refused changes left out), so an attendee's task stays paused and an absent agent is notified until they revalidate. M04's note said these were the caller's list until M02/M06; the caller now passes this list.
- **Lease.** `sweep(project)` (owner, host, service) closes votes whose time is up on what was cast, fails ties whose casting window is over, and ends records nobody finished two hours after the meeting's end (as `interrupted`, motions void). Each step has a command ID derived from the record and its state, so repeating the sweep does each step once. Nothing calls `sweep` by itself: the controller's loop must, with `tick` and `prepare`.
- **Decisions I made (for the owner to confirm).** (1) Quorum counts abstentions as present and counts the whole roster in the denominator. (2) A chair on the roster who cast no ballot may cast the casting vote (treated like an abstainer). (3) Nobody voting yes or no (all abstain) fails without a casting vote. (4) Votes last 10 minutes by default (up to 120) and the casting window is the same length; the lease is two hours after the meeting's end. (5) "Effective authority" is read as: the carrying agent holds the action under its grants and restrictions at the time of applying, the agenda request type allows action, and no owner decision stands on the topic. (6) The owner's presence is judged when the vote concludes. (7) Seconding is not built (the rule is unset in the owner decisions). (8) A vote can be revised only after an owner message, as in M06's text. (9) A tabled motion has no decision record; it is listed under unresolved.
- **Limits stated.** 1,000 entries per meeting (50 more for system entries), 30 motions, 20 versions and 60 trail entries per motion, 100 tagged items, 100 corrected entries, 10 changes per motion, 100 open records per project (more is `unavailable`, never a silent truncation). Appends to one meeting contend on its record head; contention is retried (12 times) and then surfaced as `stale_revision`. The summary reads every motion, decision and the roster's ballots on each read (bounded by the limits). Only `task.update` has an applier; other kinds (plans, contracts, priorities) are registered by the controller when their services are wired. There is no state-API or chat operation for any of this yet, no notification to the owner of a decision waiting for them (U11's kinds are tied to records), no `Needs you` item, and M03's stand-up agenda does not yet read `latestPlan` (it is stored and readable; the agenda preparation should show it). `returnOutcomes` uses M01's `setAgendaState`, so it needs the owner or a `meeting.manage` principal; it resolves an item the motion answered (outcome link = the decision record, note in words) or defers it with a reason when nothing was decided. A meeting record does not settle the M01 occurrence, and `MeetingService.settle` does not close a record: the procedure calls both.
- **What later cards must know.** M05/M06: open the record when the meeting starts, write each turn with `append` (as the speaker's own principal, or `speaker` from the controller), run the objection check and rounds outside this service, `openVote` / `castBallot` / `closeVote`, `hold` and `resume` for the owner's inquiry period, `finish` then `MeetingService.settle` then `summarize` and `returnOutcomes`; call `sweep` from the controller loop. The chair's meeting-scoped desk read is the one allowed exception to "rationale grants no access" and belongs to M05. A wiring card must register appliers and pass `authority` (the principal service's lookup passed through `GrantService.effective`) and `shareable` (shared notes tier and artifacts true, desk and scratch false).
- **Mutation checks.** Each of these was broken in turn and made at least one test fail: ballots shown to everyone while open; a non-roster agent voting; an absent member voting; quorum ignored; the chair's own ballot ignored on a tie; a tie with no chair adopted; anyone casting the casting vote; a changed chair still casting; ballots of an old version counted; a quiet non-material rewrite during voting; revising without an owner message; a held vote taking ballots; late ballots accepted; apply skipping the revision check; apply skipping the authority check; scope ignoring the carriers' authority; scope ignoring the agenda item's request type; scope ignoring an owner decision on the topic; owner presence not making a decision wait; a pending decision applying; anyone answering for the owner; apply open to any principal; citations leaking to readers; private sources treated as shareable; a citation shown to a reader; an unapplied change listed as applied; any viewer reading a record; the sweep leaving expired votes; the lease not ending abandoned records; ending a meeting leaving a vote open; a failing summarizer aborting the summary; the plan not kept; an owner message forged by an agent; an agent writing as another; the owner's alternative not being a C08 record; the never-list off; no deferral when too few can vote; an implementation hold ignored; progress not saved before the effect; the sweep reporting ties as closed; the repeat of an append making a second entry. No survivors. Not mutation-tested: the rule that every ballot also moves the motion's revision (so a close racing a ballot retries); there is no seam to interleave them, so it rests on the design and the parallel-ballots test.

**M02 review round 1 changes.**
- **Chair of record.** Only the chair the vote opened under counts: a chair named or changed since (or no chair at the open) gets neither its own ballot as the tie-breaker nor a casting vote, and the tie fails. Tests for a changed chair and a chair named after the ballots.
- **Ballots stay hidden until a tally exists.** `ballots()` shows every ballot only when the motion has a concluded decision; while a vote is open, and for a voided, tabled or amended-then-tabled motion, the owner included sees none (lead decision), a voter sees its own. The summary's attendance counts voters only from concluded motions, so who has voted does not leak while open.
- **Owner decisions cannot be dodged.** The topic check covers the motion's topic and the agenda item's own topic, and a motion with changes must name a topic or an agenda item (refused otherwise). Lead decision: an owner decision on a topic counts for a motion whether or not the motion names it, via its agenda item.
- **Summary `consistent`** now checks something: each change of each adopted decision is in exactly one of applied, not applied or waiting, nothing else is listed, and `adopted` is exactly the adopted decisions (`isConsistent`, tested including a broken input).
- **`speaker`** is refused on rulings and owner messages.
- **Owner arrival before apply.** An owner who joins while an adopted in-scope decision is not yet applied makes it `pending` (it waits for them); a decision already applied is only reported. Owner presence is otherwise judged when the vote concludes (accepted).
- **Stated, not changed.** Applied changes are made as the applying owner/host/service actor (the audit shows that actor, the authority checked is the carrier's). An owner's `alternative` is recorded in C08 before the meeting decision is updated, so a concurrent decline could leave an owner decision with no meeting decision pointing at it (it is a valid owner decision on its own). The C08 read in `#ownerDecisionOn` uses an internal owner-shaped principal; a service-read seam in `DecisionService` was not added. Provisional lead decisions: quorum counting, a rostered chair without a ballot may cast, all-abstain fails, 10-minute limits, no seconding.

## H12 notes: the local worker runtime and the attempt dispatcher (2026-10-23)

`packages/controller/src/dispatch/` (`dispatcher.ts` the service and its tick, `tools.ts` the tool path, `model.ts` records, `ports.ts` the dispatch contract, `local-worker.ts`, `loops.ts`, `cache.ts`, `network.ts`, `testing/world.ts` the test composition, exported through `@nimblenewt/controller/testing`), plus `packages/worker/src/sandbox/attempt.ts` and `packages/integration/src/h12-dispatch.test.ts`. Tests: `dispatcher.test.ts` 24, `local-worker.test.ts` 5, `loops.test.ts` 1, `cache.test.ts` 4, `network.test.ts` 4 (the first three on each of the four backends), `attempt.test.ts` 2, the integration test 1 (a real Pi turn with the scripted offline model), one added to `admission.test.ts` (16 -> 17 per backend). New ID prefixes `dpa` (dispatch), `top` (tool operation); new blocker types `grant_needed` and `dispatch_blocked`.

**Owner decision carried (2026-10-22).** The dispatcher issues the task credential itself, with no owner step. `TaskCredentialService.issueForAttempt` is callable only by the principal `service:dispatcher` (an enrolled `service`, checked against the stored record and its current credential), only for the task's active attempt at the current generation held by an active agent, on an open task. The credential is the agent's own grants cut to the task's project and task (as before), and, when the contract has a permission list, its `tool.*` actions are cut to that list (`class.X` entries expand to the catalogue's tools of that class; `Principal.taskScope.limit`, enforced in `allows`/`allowsExactly`). The owner path (`issue`) is unchanged. The issue is recorded (`issuedVia: dispatcher`, `issuedBy`, `attemptId`, `limit`; plus the command's audit entry); it ends with the task, with the attempt (`endForAttempt`), at its expiry and with the agent. An owner question is raised only when the contract names a full `tool.<name>` the agent does not hold: a `grant_needed` blocker (basis enforced permission, resolver owner) plus a `blocker` notification if a notification service is given. U11b (Needs-you) is not built; the blocker is what it will read.

**The tick** (`DispatcherService.tick`, injected clock, no sleeps): (1) ends attempts whose lease or expiry ran out (calls settled from the ledger, place and credential given back, attempt finished `abandoned`, and, if the attempt was lost, the next attempt admitted by the dispatcher up to the contract's `maxAttempts` or 3; then a `dispatch_blocked` blocker); (2) reads `ContractService.priorityOf` for every waiting request and calls the new `AdmissionService.reprioritize` (batch, waiting entries only, arrival order kept, promotes in the same step; nothing in the contract is written); (3) advances claimed attempts; (4) scans a page of assignments (in-memory cursor, `complete` says the scan reached the end) for active attempts without a dispatch, runs the checks and claims them; (5) looks at calls waiting for an approval; (6) runs the injected loops. `idle()` waits for the turns it started.

**Order of the checks before a claim:** generation current; pause gates (L01); owner decisions and gates (C08 `guard(task,"start")`); meeting revalidation and a coming meeting (M04, optional); route and harness (the resolver, normally R03 `resolve`, with the decision keyed by the revisions it read) and the F08 recovery gate (`HarnessCatalog.checkAttempt`; an attempt naming no harness is refused unless the task needs no recovery); budget from accounting (new `BudgetService.accounting`, owner/host/service only); the grants the contract names; a host that is enrolled, not revoked, fresh and has the harness (`HostService.status` now also answers the dispatcher). A passing reason skips (reported, nothing held); a lasting one opens a blocker naming the owner. Then H03: the request carries `priorityOf`, and a start is refused unless the request is running right then, the host is still fresh and the generation still current. The attempt record (`dispatch`) holds harness, route, role, routing/profiles/policy (installation, project)/decision-context/contract/environment revisions, host, request, worker version, `expiresAt`, lease and heartbeat count. `InboxService` gained `harness` (option and per call) so every admission names it, and a refused harness is now thrown with its reason instead of retried.

**The tool path** (`ToolPath`, handed to the worker as a `ToolGateway` bound to the attempt; never exposed to agents or the state API). Every call gets a durable `tool-op` record whose ID derives from attempt and call ID (asking again is the same call; a different tool or arguments under that ID is `command_conflict`): tool name and exact version, MCP server with protocol and catalogue revision and the T03 call ID, argument digest, size and redacted summary (nothing for a `restricted` call, or for its result), `readOnly` (the catalogue says `inspect`, never the worker's word), state (`awaiting-approval`, `in-progress`, `confirmed`, `failed`, `cancelled`, `uncertain`, `refused`; the first and last are additions to the card's list), start, end, diagnostics, duration (injected clock), output bytes and the CPU/memory the executor reported, the policy decision and the revisions judged under. Judgement order per call: fence (a stale generation throws `fenced` and records nothing), frozen, pause gates, owner decisions/gates, the generation hold, the decision-context version the attempt started under (a change refuses every call until the worker calls `acknowledgeDecisions`), route still eligible (R01 rules re-run on the recorded route), budget, the agent's grants as they are now (the credential snapshot is retaken per call, so a grant change applies at the next call with no restart), then `PolicyService.evaluate` (only that; `spentSoFar` from accounting). `allow` runs; `deny` refuses; `agent-review`/`owner` creates an A05 review for the exact operation and parks the call: the call returns `awaiting-approval`, and the worker makes the same call again with the same call ID once approved (the dispatcher tells the worker through `handle.tell`); the approval is checked again against everything above, used with `ReviewService.admit`, and a call the policy sent to the owner is run only if the owner decided. **Decision (conflict in the card text):** the contract's permission list is read per call; a call inside it is covered by the approved contract (policy scope), a call **beyond** it is not refused but goes to the owner (the card's "only an action beyond the ceiling asks again"); the credential's own limit stays hard for what the credential presents. Tools of the classes inspect, workspace-edit and unknown may run as local executors; every other class must name an effect executor (kind, target, input, bridge) and runs through `EffectService.intend`/`run` with `beforeStart` = pause + decisions + generation hold; a local executor for such a tool is refused.

**Kill and recovery.** A call is recorded `in-progress` before it runs. After a kill, the lost-attempt path (or `recover`) settles each open call: an effect from the ledger (confirmed/failed/uncertain copied; started but unrecorded: the target is asked through the bridge registered for the effect kind in `bridges`, never executed blindly; never started: cancelled); a read-only local call: cancelled (it can be made again); any other local call: uncertain. `settle(cancel)` aborts local executors and gives cooperative ones five event-loop turns; an executor that ignores the signal is not waited for (its call is settled from what is known and whatever it returns later changes nothing). A call on the ledger is never cut.

**Parking and places.** When a turn ends with every remaining call waiting for an approval, the attempt gives its place back (`parked`), keeping its checkpoint (`TurnResult.checkpoint`) and credential; when a decision comes the next tick moves it to `waiting`, it asks for a place again and the worker is started with `resumeFrom` and `resumeNote`. Test: twenty gated attempts hold no place and an unrelated task runs.

**Control.** `freeze`/`release`, `settle`, `quiesce` (freeze, settle, checkpoint: call it before integrating, refreshing or quarantining), `changeRecipe` (G01: `grants` needs nothing; `narrow`: freeze, settle, checkpoint, `handle.restart(recipe, checkpoint)` before the next action; `widen`: the same and `tell`; a failure leaves the attempt frozen), `stop` (emergency skips settle and checkpoint), `recover`, `acknowledgeDecisions`, `heartbeat`. Callers: owner/host/service or `assignment.manage` on the task. `checkpoints` is a hook (C04's `capture` is composed there).

**Local worker and the contract** (`ports.ts`, `local-worker.ts`). `WorkerHost.start(WorkOrder) -> WorkerHandle`, `run(gateway)`, `checkpoint`, `restart`, `tell`, `stop`; order, call request and results are plain JSON, so a remote worker (H02) implements the same contract (a test round-trips them through JSON and uses the split `begin`/`report` form). `LocalWorker` registers from its own facts (`capabilities()`), beats, opens a sandbox and a runtime per attempt through `SandboxPort`/`RuntimeFactory` (no sandbox: the start fails and the attempt ends failed, never unconfined), beats the lease through an injected scheduler, and restarts the sandbox and runtime from a checkpoint. `catalogFacts` builds the T01 host facts from the announced capabilities and the declared capacity, never from the caller. `attemptSandbox` (worker package) gives the recipe of an attempt (checkout, home, scratch, inputs only) and the launcher protected paths including the agent's separate git directory; a git directory the sandbox could reach is refused. `resolvePublic`/`connectPublic` look a name up once, require every address to be public and hand back the checked address to connect to (DNS rebinding).

**Loops** (`loops.ts`; the tick runs them as `service:dispatcher`): U12 `settleDue` and `release` per active agent, M04 `sweepReturns`, M03 `prepare` per planned occurrence of every project and `deliverOutcomes`, H06b `expireIdle` (repeated while `more`), C08 `resume` per project. A failing loop is reported and does not stop the others. `CachePolicies` (C06/C06b requirements): per route, derived scope/share keys, slots from the shared `CacheEndpoints` partitioned by project, pin only with an active marker, isolation requirement.

**Not done (for follow-up cards; none of the acceptance text is weakened):** (1) H02 remote workers: only the contract and its JSON round trip; no transport. (2) `LocalWorker` is bound to fake sandbox ports in tests and to the real Pi adapter only in the integration test (`SandboxPort` over `SandboxLauncher`, the recipe from A08 limits and the real worker process are not built). (3) The DNS-rebinding guard is not wired into an MCP client (none exists). (4) Nothing in A04/A06/quarantine calls `quiesce` yet. (5) Pi's HTTP calls are not routed through `CacheAdapter`, so slotted servers still run with reuse off (`degraded`). (6) C07b `ContextEnforcementService.admit`/`resumeDecision` are not called at start or resume. (7) `PauseService.observe` (reporting `paused`) is not wired. (8) U12 boundary reads (`readable(...,"interrupt")`) are not delivered to the runtime. (9) `start()` does not build the dispatcher; the composer builds it with `DispatcherService.withEpoch` (see `testing/world.ts`). (10) After a failed (not lost) attempt no new attempt starts by itself; a blocker names the owner. (11) U11b Needs-you. (12) Owner-private decisions: the approval policy's "owner" mode is enforced by requiring the deciding principal to be the owner; the review service itself can still route such a review to an agent. (13) The dispatch record's `inFlight` list serialises calls of one attempt (32 at most). (14) The scan cursor is in memory (a restart rescans). (15) Policy `spentSoFar` is in the unit of `costMicros`; contract `maxCost` has no stated unit.

**Decisions I made:** the beyond-ceiling rule above; an empty permission list states no limit; a worker's turn that ends with parked calls yields its place; a lost attempt is re-admitted up to 3 attempts; a decision-context change blocks all calls until acknowledged; `status` of a host answers `service:dispatcher`.

**H12 review round 1 changes (2026-10-23).**
- An effect call recorded on the ledger is only ever continued through the ledger: asked again with a local executor, or through `begin`, it is settled from the ledger and never handed back to run (the dead `resume` branch is removed). Two asks for one call ID are one call: in one process the second joins the first; across dispatchers the create of the operation is the claim and the loser is treated as asking again.
- An attempt is started once: the update to `running` throws unless the dispatch is still `waiting` with the request this caller holds, and the worker is started only after that commit; `tick()` is not reentrant (a second call gets the first's report). Updates carry a per-dispatcher instance in their command ID: two dispatchers that read one revision no longer share a command (found by a probe: the second was answered with the first's result). The place and host checks live in `#advance`/`#checks`, which run again for every waiting claim; `#start` no longer repeats them (tested: a withdrawn place gets a new request, a stale host is skipped).
- A parked attempt resumes whenever any parked call's review has been decided, whether or not the worker was told; the told set is cleared on yield and bounded.
- `ToolGateway.acknowledgeDecisions()` clears a decision-revision refusal for a worker, remote-shaped ones included; `LocalWorker` refreshes the agent's context (`AttemptRuntime.refreshContext`) and acknowledges after such a refusal (the refused call stays refused; the agent calls again under a new ID).
- Grant checks (per call, control, read, the grant snapshot) pass the injected clock. A priority that returns to an earlier value is applied again: only entries whose stored priority differs are sent, keyed by the queue revision. `merged()` raises `integrity` if two guards create one record with different contents; `#requireDispatcher` needs the credential and `asOf`; reasons shown to the worker are redacted.
- Settled local calls are recorded in the tool operations (`tool-op-*`), not in the S05 ledger; only effects are in the ledger.
- Lead decisions: a call beyond the contract's permission ceiling goes to the owner; an empty permission list means the credential carries the agent's own grants cut to project and task and nothing more; an exact-operation review (A05: a call that carries a script, or an install) inside an approved ceiling goes to the owner, while a policy-level agent review stays with any reviewer; contract `budget.maxCost` is in the same micro-cost unit as the spend the policy is given (`costMicros`). The A10b sentence "tasks within one project are not separated by the credential" holds for checks that name no task (project-wide grants are held through any task's credential); a check that names another task is still refused, as A10b has it.
- `attemptSandbox` is not wired into `LocalWorker` (the controller cannot import the worker package): follow-up H12b. Follow-ups: H12a H02 transport; H12b bind A03 launcher, A08 recipe, `attemptSandbox` and the worker process; H12c compose the dispatcher in `start()` with a durable scan cursor; H12d `quiesce` into A04/A06/quarantine; H12e Pi HTTP through `CacheAdapter`; H12f C07b admit/resumeDecision, L01 observe, U12 boundary reads; H12g DNS-rebinding guard into the remote MCP client; U11b depends on H12; owner-mode review routing depends on A05.

## H12h notes: owner-mode reviews go only to the owner (2026-10-22)

- What it is: the review service itself now keeps a review in owner mode away from agent reviewers. Owner mode is any review whose body is `owner-decision`, whose route is `mandatoryHuman` or `escalated` (reserved by the policy, sent to the owner by a reviewer's `requires-owner-decision`, or escalated by a missed deadline). The dispatcher already accepted only the owner's decision; this closes the gaps behind it.
- Gaps closed (packages/controller/src/review/review.ts, helper `ownerMode`): (1) `decide` refuses a non-owner for an owner-mode review whatever its state, so an owner `request-context` plus the requester's `supplyContext` can no longer turn it into a `pending` review that the agent still named on the route may decide; (2) `supplyContext` returns an owner-mode review (or one whose tool is now reserved) to `owner-decision`, never `pending`; (3) `addContributor` no longer reassigns an owner-mode review to another agent; (4) `get` and `getRoute` do not show an owner-mode review (or one whose tool is reserved now) to an agent reviewer; the requester, whoever decided it and the owner still see it; (5) `wakeList` and `waitEdges` skip owner-mode reviews and pending ones whose tool the policy has reserved since routing, so no agent is woken or waited on before the next sweep.
- Not done: no data migration; an old route that kept `assigned` after `requires-owner-decision` keeps that field (it is ignored). An agent who escalated a review can no longer read it afterwards (conservative).
- Tests: packages/controller/src/review/owner-mode.test.ts (4 tests on each of the 4 backends): every route (request, decide with all four verdicts, supplyContext, addContributor, sweep, get/getRoute, wakeList, waitEdges, admit) for policy-reserved, reviewer-escalated, deadline-escalated and reserved-later reviews, and a control that agent-mode reviews are unchanged.

### H12h follow-up (review round 1)
- `RequestReview.ownerOnly` (optional boolean): the review is created in owner mode (route `escalated` and `mandatoryHuman`, body `owner-decision`, nobody assigned), exactly as for a tool the policy reserves. It can only make a review stricter, so the requester or the dispatcher may set it; it never loosens policy. A repeat request is cleared only by an owner approval, as for reserved tools. `ToolGateway.#park` (dispatch/tools.ts) now passes `ownerOnly` for every dispatcher-owner-only call (exact operation inside the ceiling, beyond the ceiling, policy mode owner), so an agent reviewer is no longer assigned, woken, shown or able to decide it; the dispatcher's later check of the approver stays as a second line.
- `DecisionService.#cannotClear` refuses any non-owner who tries to revalidate a review link when the review is in owner mode or was decided by the owner: an agent reviewer can no longer unblock admission of an owner-approved operation after its decision was superseded (forbidden; the owner revalidates).
- Owner attention queue: before this card no path created a `review-request` notification when a review was created (the notification kind and `emitFromController` existed, nothing called them for reviews). The dispatcher now emits one (`dpa-rvn:<reviewId>`, best effort) when it parks an owner-only call. Reviews made by other callers (the commit gate, a direct `request`) and reviews escalated later by a sweep or by a reviewer's `requires-owner-decision` still raise no notification: follow-up (U11b should list reviews in `owner-decision` directly).

## H12c notes: the dispatcher composed at controller start, the driver and the durable scan cursor (2026-10-23)

`packages/controller/src/api/dispatch-compose.ts` (the composer), `dispatch/driver.ts` (`DispatchDriver`, `TimerPort`, `realTimer`), `dispatch/testing/timer.ts` (`ManualTimer`), changes in `dispatch/dispatcher.ts`, `api/start.ts`, `security/principals.ts`, `decisions/decisions.ts`, `meetings/meetings.ts`. Tests: `dispatch/cursor.test.ts` 3 and `dispatch/driver.test.ts` 8 (each on the four backends), `api/start-dispatch.test.ts` 4 (SQLite), one added to `meetings.test.ts` and an extension of one in `decisions.test.ts`.

**What start does now.** `StartOptions.dispatch` (absent: nothing is built, nothing runs, existing behaviour). When given, `startController` builds, on the controller's one epoch, the services the dispatcher needs that it did not have (admission, hosts, effects, blockers, policy, budgets, notifications, pauses, events, decisions, generation, difficulty/route, meetings, messages, attendance, stand-ups), the loops (messages `settleDue` and `release` per active agent; attendance `sweepReturns`; stand-ups `prepare` and `deliverOutcomes`; events `expireIdle`; decisions `resume`) and the `DispatchDriver`; it starts the driver unless `manual: true`. `Controller.dispatch` holds `{ dispatcher, driver }`; `dispatcher.gateway(attemptId)` is the acknowledgement/heartbeat/call path the worker hosts given in `dispatch.workers` receive. `dispatch.clock` is passed to the services built for the dispatcher and the driver; the services start.ts built before keep the real clock (a test that needs one clock for everything uses the world in `dispatch/testing/world.ts`).

**The dispatcher principal.** `PrincipalService.provisionService("dispatcher")` (no caller, no API operation; only code that builds the services can call it) creates `service:dispatcher` if absent (no grants; it keeps any grants the owner later gives it) and gives it a fresh credential held only in the process (no secret on disk, nothing for the owner to do). A later start rotates it, as it takes the epoch. A dispatcher the owner disabled is not re-enabled: start prints "The dispatcher is not running: ..." and sets `Controller.dispatchProblem`; the rest of the controller starts.

**The driver.** One tick at a time from a `TimerPort` (`realTimer`: `setTimeout`, unref'ed, re-armed after the tick ends; tests give `ManualTimer`, which fires when told and resolves when the tick ended). Before every tick it compares `storage.currentEpoch()` with the controller's: a different one means another instance took over, and the driver is **fenced** for good (no tick, no timer, no write, not even its health record); a `stale_epoch` thrown inside a tick fences it too. A tick that throws is retried with a doubling delay up to one minute and the counter resets on a good tick. Errors are never silent: the last 50 are kept in `status().errors` (older ones counted in `dropped`, text cut at 300 characters), each goes to `onInternalError`, loop failures reported inside a tick are counted by name (`loopFailures`), and the same bounded list is written to the `dispatch-health/loops` record (best effort; a write that fails is passed to the observer). `stop()` (called by `Controller.close()` before the listener and the store) cancels the timer, waits for the tick running, calls `DispatcherService.shutdown()` and then `idle()`.

**Shutdown (round 2).** `DispatcherService.shutdown()` takes each attempt that is *running* here: its calls are settled, a checkpoint taken, its worker ended, its place given back, and the same attempt goes back to `waiting` with `resumeFrom`, so a restart neither admits a new attempt nor counts against `maxAttempts`, and opens no blocker (three restart cycles are a test). Waiting and parked attempts are not touched: a parked attempt's pending approvals stay pending and still resume it afterwards. `dropWorkers()` only ends the local worker handles with no store write. A fenced driver calls `dropWorkers()` at fence time and never calls `shutdown()` (nor at `stop()`); a stop that finds the fence during its final tick keeps `fenced`.

**Driver round 2.** Errors are grouped by source and message (`count`, `firstAt`, last `at`), so a permanent failure is one list entry. The `dispatch-health` record is written when a new kind of error appears, otherwise at most once a minute, and at stop. Before each tick the driver also checks that `service:dispatcher` is still active; if the owner disabled it, the driver closes its attempts, stops for good (`disabled`), records it and files a `maintenance` Notice for the owner (also when start finds it disabled). A routine credential rotation at start is not reported.

**The durable cursor.** The scan position is the aggregate `dispatch-cursor/scan` (`{ after }`; empty when a pass ended). The first scan of a process reads it; later scans use the in-memory position. It is written after the page was worked (never before), when the page had attempts in it, when a pass ended, and otherwise at most once a minute (a position that moves over many assignments does not cost a write every tick), so a crash in between rescans the page: an attempt that already has its dispatch record is skipped, and one not yet claimed is claimed. A restart goes on from where the last tick stopped. Assignments behind the cursor that become active later are found on the next pass, as before.

**One bad attempt no longer stops a tick.** A claim or start that throws (found in the start test: a meeting revalidation refuses an agent ID of the wrong shape) is reported in `TickReport.failures` (new field) and the scan goes on; the driver records each failure as an error (`attempt <id>`). Without this a poison attempt would stop every tick before the cursor moved. A `stale_epoch` still aborts the tick.

**Authorization found missing when the loops ran against real services (they were mocked in `loops.test.ts`), fixed minimally and tested.** These two edits are identity exemptions for `service:dispatcher`, not grants (a service cannot hold them). (1) `DecisionService.resume`/`propagate` refused `service:dispatcher` (a service may hold only recovery grants): `#mayPass` now names the dispatcher service for `assignment.manage`. (2) `MeetingService.planned` returned `undefined` for it, so `standups.prepare` would silently have prepared nothing: `planned` lets the dispatcher service list (any other service still reads like a missing project).

**Not wired / limits.** Without `dispatch.workers` attempts are admitted and checked but nothing starts (blocker or skip says why). No default `route`: the owner's execution profiles and routing table decide (R03); until they are set a ready attempt reports a `route` blocker. `ReviewService` is the one start.ts already built (without the decision service; the dispatcher does not need it more than before). Attendance `wake`/`notices` sinks and stand-up `notices`/`outcomes` sinks are not given (optional in those services; a conversation/inbox sink is not built yet): invitations and notices are recorded but not delivered to a channel. `PauseService.availability` is used for the new meeting services while `options.availability` (default: everyone available) still feeds the conversation service. The dispatcher's other tick phases (leases, approvals) still stop the tick on an unexpected error; the driver retries with backoff. The driver's health record is written only on errors. The H12b/H12f items (real sandbox, quiesce, boundary delivery to attempts) are not touched.
## H02 notes: subordinate remote work and artifact transport (2026-10-23)

`packages/controller/src/remote/` (`protocol.ts` versions, limits and wire shapes; `service.ts` `RemoteWorkerService`, the controller's logic; `server.ts` `WorkerTransport`, the HTTP(S) carrier; `client.ts` `RemoteWorkerClient`/`RemoteAttempt`, the worker's side; `testing/` the test world). Small edits in `dispatch/` (below). New record kinds `remote-session` (one per host), `remote-placement` (one per attempt), `remote-store` (one per agent). Tests: `remote.test.ts` 10, `transport.test.ts` 8 (+2 backend-free), `artifacts.test.ts` 4, `relocation.test.ts` 12 (all but the two backend-free ones on each of the four backends), two added to `dispatcher.test.ts` and one assertion to the parked-attempt test.

- **The H12 contract crosses unchanged.** `WorkOrder`, `ToolCallRequest`, `ToolResult`, `WorkerTurnResult`, `TurnUsage` and the split `begin`/`report` form are the wire messages (plain JSON). `RemoteAttempt` implements `ToolGateway`, so code written for the contract runs over the wire; its `call` runs local executors through `begin`/`report` and refuses an effect executor. Every call goes to the dispatcher's own gateway for the attempt (same checks, same records, same generation fence). **Limit:** a tool that acts on the outside (effect class) is refused from a remote worker (`refused`: it needs the ledger and a bridge, which the controller runs); a controller-run effect form of the contract is a follow-up (H12a decides).
- **Protocol version.** Integer range per side (`WORKER_PROTOCOL` 1..1 now; the controller's range is an option for tests and later versions). `connect` sends the worker's range; the highest common version is the session's, every later request names it (`x-newt-protocol`) and a different one is refused (426). No common version: the worker gets 426 `protocol_unsupported` naming what the controller speaks and which side is too old, and the refusal is written on the host's `remote-session` record (`session`, `refused` for the owner); it has no session and nothing it sends is accepted. A later successful connect clears the refusal.
- **Authentication and admission of a worker.** Every request authenticates afresh (`PrincipalService.authenticate`); only principals of kind `host` are accepted (owner, agent, client, service credentials get 403); a revoked host or an old credential is refused on its next request (and a stale `Principal` object is refused by the service, which re-reads the stored credential). The listener is loopback by default; remote exposure needs `allowRemote`, TLS and `publicHosts` (a worker's credential must not cross a network in the clear); the `Host` header must be the listener's own (DNS rebinding); bodies, rates (per host and per address, injected clock) and connections are bounded. `connect` takes the host's capabilities from the body through `HostService.announce` (its own validation, bounds, secret-shape refusal): they are the host's own registration, never a name the caller gives. The session epoch is the session record's revision, so it only grows; the same idempotency key is the same session. Heartbeats go to `HostService.heartbeat`.
- **No self-promotion, no independent work.** A worker has no route that admits work, creates or changes an attempt or task, or reads a controller record; bodies with fields a route does not define are refused (so a worker cannot name a project, task or creator). Work reaches a host only as an offer the dispatcher made (`place`, owner/dispatcher only, and only when the dispatcher's own record says the attempt runs on that host at that generation). The worker accepts exactly its offer; anything about an attempt placed on another host reads as missing (404); a former holder is told `fenced`.
- **Fencing under partition and delay.** Every message about an attempt is checked against the placement: the host must be the holder, the **placement number** must be the current one, the **session** must be the one that accepted it (a reconnect makes the worker `accept` again, which is also where it learns that the attempt moved or ended), the state must be live and the attempt unexpired. So a packet delayed past a reconnect, a restart of the attempt on the same host, a relocation, the end of the attempt or the lease running out cannot act. A silent worker's attempt is lost by the dispatcher's lease as before; its placement ends with the run (`end`, which takes the run's number so the stop of an earlier run cannot end a later one). Checkpoints carry a sequence: an older one is refused, the same message again is the same checkpoint; `complete` repeated with the same result is the same answer.
- **Artifact fetch and publish.** A worker lists and fetches only the artifacts its placement was given (inputs), published itself (outputs) or its checkpoint, each read through `ArtifactService.readFile` (hash and size verified; a damaged blob is a generic `integrity` error and nothing of the bytes) with the digest in `x-newt-digest`, which the client checks again. One file per fetch up to 16 MiB (larger is refused with its size, not cut: chunking is a follow-up). A worker publishes up to 8 MiB (base64 in JSON) as an artifact of the placement's own project and task with the host as creator; at most 50 per placement. Giving a worker an input that fails `assertResumable` refuses the placement.
- **Checkpoints are artifacts.** A checkpoint (at most 1 MiB, canonical JSON) is stored as a `checkpoint.json` artifact, so it is content-addressed and verified on every read; 200 per attempt.
- **Relocation** (`relocate`, owner or dispatcher; the command has no field for policy). Order: judge the target (active, fresh, connected, platform, harness, isolation, workspace bindings, credential policy, data location; all reasons are listed), verify what is stored, ask the old host for a checkpoint with calls settled (`DispatcherService.quiesce`; an old host that does not answer is fenced anyway with its last stored checkpoint), verify again, then **one commit**: placement number +1 (the old host is fenced from here), state `relocating`, the native store handed to the target; then `DispatcherService.relocate` (settles calls in flight, gives the place back, waits pinned to the target with the checkpoint as `resumeFrom` and a note); the normal tick starts it there and nowhere else. Everything that can refuse comes before the commit and leaves the old host running (the freeze is lifted). A move stopped after the commit is finished by repeating the command ID. Defaults (the controller's `relocationPolicy` hook can widen): same platform as the host it was placed on; every enrolled host may hold the credential; **`local-only` data is never moved unless the policy lists the target**. An attempt that made calls and has no checkpoint is not moved.
- **Native Pi store.** One owner host per agent (`remote-store`), claimed by the first placement; an attempt of that agent is placed only there, and only one at a time (a live attempt holds it; a placement past its expiry does not). It moves with `relocate` (same commit as the fence) or the owner's `moveStore` (refused while an attempt is live, target must be connected and fresh). A parked attempt now resumes on the host it was on (`pinnedHost`, set when it yields). **Limit:** the record is the controller's guarantee of exclusivity; the store's files are not touched.
- **Edits to H12 code (tested):** `DispatchBody.pinnedHost`; `#pickHost` honours it (never falls back); `#yield` sets it; `#start` clears it; `DispatcherService.relocate` (owner/dispatcher only); the turn's finalizer ignores a result when its handle is no longer the attempt's live one, and a finished turn removes only its own live entry (a stale old turn used to be able to end or unlist a restarted attempt).
- **Not done / limits:** (1) H12a: the product `WorkerHost`/`WorkerHandle` over this (the tests use `RemoteTestHost`): `handle.checkpoint()` must reject when the worker does not answer (relocation relies on it; no timer is used here), `stop()` should pass the run's placement to `end`, `run()` is `waitForResult`. (2) Offers hold the task credential in memory only; a controller restart loses an unaccepted offer and the attempt's lease ends. (3) Effect tools from remote workers (above). (4) The `ToolCallRequest` flags the policy reads (`irreversible`, `estimatedSpend`, ...) are the worker's words, as for a local worker. (5) A call that passed the placement check just before a relocation can still land (it settles through the ledger); the window is one request. (6) No chunked or resumable transfer; no client-side retry delay (a request with no answer is repeated at once, twice, with its key). (7) `moveStore` does not check platform or data policy (it is the owner's explicit act). (8) Stale offers for ended attempts are dropped lazily.

**H02 review round 1 changes (2026-10-23).**
- Concurrent writes to one placement are all applied: the command ID of an internal write is random per call and its payload carries a digest of the new body (before, two writes of one kind at one revision looked like a replay and the second was reported as done without being applied). Tests on all backends: concurrent `control` and concurrent `publish`.
- Relocation with a worker that answers: after `quiesce` the placement is read again; the worker's own checkpoint (matched by digest) is used, otherwise the fresh one is stored after the latest. `complete` with a checkpoint stores it once however often it is repeated. The test handle now makes the worker send its checkpoint over the wire.
- A repeated relocation is no longer recognised by its reason. A move to a target that is still `relocating` is finished by any repeat; once the target has taken the attempt up only the command that made the move is answered again (and does nothing); `DispatcherService.relocate` is a no-op for an attempt already running on that host. `lastRelocation` records the command.
- The dispatcher knows the store owner (`DispatcherDeps.storeOwner`, bound by the composer to `RemoteWorkerService.storeOwner`): the agent's attempts are started on the owner host only, and an attempt whose store is held by another attempt waits (skipped, no blocker, no failure) and runs once the first ends. `place()` stays as the backstop.
- Client: the certificate is checked against the host in the URL (no `servername` override); `http:` is accepted only for loopback; request timeout (30 s) and response cap (32 MiB) options. Transport: at most 100 connections per address (`connectionsPerAddress`). `selfSigned` can make certificates for another name/without the IP (test support).
- A parked attempt whose host is gone: `relocate` accepts an ended placement when the dispatcher record is `parked`, using the checkpoint it parked with. A pinned attempt whose host stays unavailable gets a `dispatch_blocked` blocker naming the owner after `pinnedStaleTicks` consecutive ticks (default 5; counted in memory, a restart starts again).
- Policy defaults stated: `credentialHosts` absent or empty means any enrolled host, a non-empty list is the only hosts; `local-only` data never moves unless `dataHosts` lists the target. `DispatcherService.relocate` is the owner's override and checks no policy; the policy lives in `RemoteWorkerService.relocate`. Effect-class tools stay refused from remote workers until H12a; unaccepted offers stay in memory only; size caps without chunking stand. Not done: a separate worker entry point or lint to stop a worker process importing the controller's storage (the client imports none, tested).
- (Round 2.) The relocation commit now sits inside the try that lifts the freeze, so any failure of it (lost race, store-owner change, storage error) leaves the attempt running and not frozen; a command ID that made an earlier move (kept on the placement history) is refused with `command_conflict` before anything is judged or quiesced. Two live dispatchers that both see the store as not busy would be a split-brain; it is covered by epoch fencing (only the primary's writes commit), and `place()` re-checks the store at the commit.

## H12e notes: Pi's model calls go through the cache adapter (2026-10-23)

New: `packages/controller/src/dispatch/pi-fetch.ts` (`piFetch`, `responseBody`), `pi-fetch.test.ts` (10), `packages/integration/src/h12e-pi-cache.test.ts` (3, the real Pi adapter and pi-ai's openai-completions provider and SDK against the C06 llama.cpp simulator; only the network is replaced). Changed: `dispatch/cache.ts` (`CachePolicies`), `inference-cache/shaping.ts` (`piCachePolicy` option `routed`, `PiCachePolicy.fetch`), `inference-cache/simulators.ts` (`simulatedFetch`, exported through `@nimblenewt/controller/testing`), `packages/pi-adapter/src/cache-shaping.ts` (`ModelCachePolicy.fetch`), `dispatch/dispatcher.ts` (`#usage`). Tests added or changed: `cache.test.ts` (4 -> 6), `shaping.test.ts` (+1), `prompt-cache.test.ts` (+1), `dispatcher.test.ts` (4 added per backend). No new dependency.

- **How the call is routed.** pi-ai's provider SDKs take `options.fetch` (openai-completions, anthropic-messages and the others pass it through `simple-options`). The policy's `fetch(inner)` wrapper is installed by `shapeModels` for every model call, and for the one-shot compaction call (`completeSimple`) too. For a JSON `POST` the wrapper parses the body Pi built and calls **`CacheAdapter.call`** (not a copy of it): slot turn (`EndpointState.slot`, or `exclusive` for a call that names no slot), last-holder check and clearing (`/slots/N?action=erase`, or reuse off if the clear fails), `profile.prepare` (`cache_prompt`, `id_slot`, `cache_salt`, `strata_prefix` only when `pinAuthorized`, isolation), the plain retry when the endpoint refuses a cache setting, and the holder recorded after the call. The adapter's `Transport` is the inner fetch with Pi's own headers (its credentials) and abort signal. Anything that is not a JSON object `POST` (model lists and so on) goes to the inner fetch untouched.
- **Streaming.** A 200 `text/event-stream` answer is pumped by one reader: Pi gets its `Response` at once and reads it as it arrives, while the same reader feeds the adapter (`responseBody` merges `usage`, `timings`, Anthropic's `message.usage` and the Responses `response.usage` across events) and keeps the slot's turn until then, so no other project's call can enter the slot while it generates (tested with a gated stream). An error status or a plain JSON body is read whole and handed to Pi after the adapter has finished (a refused-setting retry shows Pi only the retry's answer). An aborted call rejects, records no holder (the next call into the slot clears it again if the last known holder was another project) and frees the turn (tested before and during the stream).
- **Same project, real reuse; other projects, none.** `CachePolicies.forAttempt` assigns the slot from the shared `SlotAffinity` as before (partitioned by project; one share key per project, so a project's agents share a slot and its prompt head) and now also builds the wrapper with the shared `EndpointState` of the endpoint URL. Tests (unit and through the real Pi adapter): the same conversation reads its own history from its slot, a second agent of the project reads the shared head, a second project with the same text reads nothing (own slot), and with one slot for two projects the second runs with `cache_prompt: false` and no `id_slot` and the first finds its slot cleared before its next call.
- **The degraded mode is only for missing state.** `piCachePolicy(..., { routed: true })` neither turns reuse off nor edits the body of a slotted server (the adapter prepares it). `CachePolicyDeps.state` is now optional: with none, or when `CacheEndpoints` cannot track another endpoint (its 64-URL bound), the route is not `routed`, the old behaviour stays (no slot named, `cache_prompt: false`, `degraded: "slotted server: the cache adapter's state is not available, so reuse is off"`) and no slot is assigned. Both are tested.
- **Hit figures reach accounting.** Every configured, non-generic route is observed (not only slotted ones): the wrapper reads the hit with the route's own profile (`cache_n`/`prompt_n`, `cached_tokens`, `cache_read_input_tokens`, which pi-ai itself does not read for llama.cpp, Radiance or Strata). `AttemptCache.takeUsage()` returns the figures since the last take as the R05 `CacheUsage` (`ledgerCache` shape: backend kind, route, cached, computed, written) and waits for calls still being judged, because the last call of a turn ends a moment after Pi has its answer. The composer puts it in the turn's `usage.cache`; **the dispatcher's `#usage` used to return before charging when a turn had no `costMicros` or `computeMs`, which dropped cache figures from a free local server; it now charges cache-only usage too** (and the command ID includes the cache figures). Tested end to end through `BudgetService.usage` (`cacheByBackend`).
- **Markers and pins.** The pin marker is resolved as before; `pinAuthorized` goes into the context the adapter prepares with, and the marker's `expectedCachedTokens` is passed to the adapter to judge each call's outcome.
- **Does not do / limits.** (1) `MarkerService.recordOutcome` is not called from the wrapper: the outcome (`hit`/`miss`) is in `PiCall.result.outcome` but a stable command ID per call needs a per-attempt sequence the dispatcher does not keep yet; a miss therefore does not yet mark a pin marker lost. (2) The composer that builds a worker's runtime (`RuntimeFactory`, H12b) must pass `attempt.policy` to the Pi adapter's `cache.policy` and `await attempt.takeUsage()` into `TurnResult.usage.cache`; the controller cannot import the Pi adapter, and the integration test does this by hand. Per route there is one `AttemptCache`; an attempt that uses several routes sums them itself. (3) Slot assignment is still made once per `forAttempt` and never released (C06 limit). (4) Calls of one project's agents into one slot take turns (a slot serves one generation at a time), so a second agent waits for the first's generation. (5) Nothing was run against a real llama-server; the stream shape (`timings` in the last chunk, `usage` with `stream_options.include_usage`) is read from its documentation; if a server omits them the hit is `unknown`, not zero. (6) The Anthropic SDK path (observe only, no adapter) is covered by the merge of events in `responseBody`, not by a request through the SDK. (7) A worker process must pass an inner fetch that is the sandbox's allowed network path; the wrapper adds no network access of its own.

**H12e review round 1 changes (2026-10-23).**
- **Usage command ID.** The `dpa-use:` ID is now attempt, heartbeat count, dispatcher instance, a per-attempt sequence and a 24-character hash of the report, so a route of any allowed length fits the 120-character limit and two identical reports are two records. A report that cannot be charged is no longer dropped silently: the attempt's dispatch record gets `usageError` (redacted, clipped). Tested with a 127-character route, two identical reports and a report that is refused. Note: `cacheByBackend.calls` in budget accounting counts usage reports (turns), not model calls.
- **Fail closed.** With the endpoint's state set, every POST is read as text whatever its shape (string, bytes, stream, blob, or a `Request` object as input with its own headers and signal) and routed through the adapter; a POST that is not a JSON object is refused with a `TypeError` and never sent. Only an observed call (no state) may pass such a request on untouched. Each shape is tested.
- **The turn ends with the answer, not the connection.** The stream is pumped by one reader: the provider's end marker (`[DONE]`, `message_stop`, `response.completed`), the stream ending, or Pi leaving (its branch's `cancel`) ends the turn at once, drops the connection (an internal `AbortController` joined with Pi's signal) and releases the slot; the hold after the marker is zero. A call that is aborted while queued for the turn is rejected at once and never sent. Tested with streams that stay open for ever.
- **Endpoint state keys** are the origin (`http://h:8080` and `http://h:8080/v1` share one `EndpointState`). The default `onError` logs (redacted).
- **Accepted by the lead / requirements for H12b.** One generation at a time per project per slot is the intended trade for prefix sharing. A miss not marking a pin lost (`MarkerService.recordOutcome`) and the accounting wiring (`attempt.policy` into the Pi adapter, `await attempt.takeUsage()` into `TurnResult.usage.cache`) are part of the H12b composer work and are required there.
- **Round 2.** The end of an answer is a whole SSE event: events are cut at their blank line, a `data:` line that is exactly `[DONE]` (or a JSON object whose own `type` is `message_stop` or `response.completed`) ends it, everything up to and including that event's blank line is forwarded to Pi, and model text that merely contains the words does not end the stream (tested, including markers split across chunks). The caller's abort listener is removed when the call finishes. `TurnUsage.turnSeq` (optional, whole number) is the idempotency key of a usage report when present: a retried report is charged once and a different report under the same number is a `command_conflict` (tested). **Requirement for H12b:** the composer/worker gateway must pass a worker-side turn sequence as `turnSeq` on every usage report, unique across all placements of one attempt (a relocated attempt keeps its ID, so a new host must continue the count, not restart it); the dispatcher's own per-attempt sequence is only an in-process fallback and does not survive a restart or a retry. **Remote workers (H02):** the remote server passes the request's `Idempotency-Key` to `gateway.usage(usage, requestKey)`; without a `turnSeq` it keys the charge, so a resent request is charged once (tested).
- **Review round 3 changes.** `piFetch` calls the caller's `onError` inside a guard (a throwing hook cannot cause an unhandled rejection); `#usageSeq` is dropped when the attempt ends; the adapter forgets a slot's holder after a successful erase. **Known, not fixed:** (a) an aborted call that still waits for the slot's turn delays `takeUsage` until its turn comes (no correctness effect); (b) `completeSimple` (compaction) does not apply `policy.payload`, so in degraded mode on a slotted server a compaction call is only observed and may reuse another project's slot (as before H12e; the routed path is correct).

## T06 notes: the protected agent tool bridge and execution service (2026-10-23)

`packages/controller/src/toolbridge/` (`service.ts` the execution service, `schema.ts` the argument checker), `packages/pi-adapter/src/bridge.ts` (the extension loaded at worker start), and three small edits to H12 (`dispatch/tools.ts`, `dispatch/dispatcher.ts`, `dispatch/model.ts`) and one to `security/task-credentials.ts`.
