Tests: `toolbridge/service.test.ts` 17 per backend (x4) plus 2 for the schema checker, `pi-adapter/src/bridge.test.ts` 8, `integration/src/t06-bridge.test.ts` 1 (a real Pi conversation). **Dependency change, loudly:** `@nimblenewt/pi-adapter` now depends on `@nimblenewt/registry` (workspace package, no third-party code) for the canonical/wire-name mapping; `package.json`, `tsconfig.json` reference, `package-lock.json` and the allowed list in `scripts/package-boundaries.mjs` were updated; the boundary check passes.

**The two halves.** The extension (`createBridge`) declares ten tools under the reserved namespace with provider-safe wire names (`nimblenewt__desk-read`, `desk-write`, `message-send`, `task-get`, `task-transition`, `task-update`, `tool-discover`, `tool-describe`, `tool-invoke`, `tool-register`), fixed text and order, all `shared` (the head of every prompt is the same), none with a `replay` flag. It holds one thing, a `BridgeTransport` (`boundTransport(token, deliver)`: a frozen object with `send` and nothing else, closed over the worker's own task credential). It has no service object and no admin method; a tampered extension can send only the closed list of requests, and the controller answers or refuses each exactly as it would for the worker. `ToolExecutionService.handle(token, gateway, request)` is the controller half. Meeting ballots, job control and environment-maintenance requests are M06, T07 and A09 (the operation list is a closed constant; they add to it).

**Who is asking (never from the caller).** A request is `{ op, callId, args }`; `onlyKnown` refuses any other field (a `principal`, `actor`, `host`, project or task is `invalid_command`), and the project and task of every desk/task/message operation are the attempt's own (an ID in the arguments is refused). The credential is authenticated (`TaskCredentialService.authenticate`), must be an agent's task credential, and its agent, task and project must equal the dispatch record of the attempt whose gateway carries the call; the attempt must be running, current (assignment generation) and not frozen. Anything else is `unauthenticated` or `fenced`. Catalogue operations are installation-wide records that a task-scoped principal cannot name, so they run as the agent with its own grants (new `TaskCredentialService.agentOf`, dispatcher-only, refused unless the attempt's credential is active); desk and message operations use the credential's task-scoped principal.

**Invoking a tool.** `tool.invoke` resolves the exact version (named, or the latest live one at this moment), refuses a revoked version, a skill, arguments that do not match the manifest's input schema (`schema.ts`: type, enum, const, properties/required/additionalProperties, items, length/size bounds, anyOf/oneOf/allOf/not, local `$ref`; a schema using a keyword it cannot enforce, such as `pattern`, is refused at registration and at use; no regular expression is ever run), a tool whose `requestedAccess` the task's credential does not hold (asking grants nothing), and a host that cannot satisfy the manifest (the host's facts come from its announcement and declared capacity, `catalogFacts`, never from the request). It then reads the exact executable bytes now (an active call finishes on that version whatever is published next) and hands the call to the dispatcher's `ToolGateway.call`, so permission, budget, pause, decisions, route, policy, approval and the durable operation are H12's, not repeated. The local executor asks `CatalogService.authorizeRun` at the moment the script starts (revocation, grants and host asked again, usage recorded; a revocation between the check and the start stops the run), then the injected `ScriptRunner`. The run is described by `ScriptLaunch`: program (the manifest's interpreter name, a plain name), `argv` = the entry point only, the model's arguments as canonical JSON on standard input, the exact source, an empty environment, the manifest's limits. No model text is ever part of a command line and nothing here builds a shell string. The output must be JSON matching the manifest's output schema, at most 64 KB.

**Versions and restart.** A call keeps the version it began with: asked again with the same call ID, the service looks up the operation (`DispatcherService.toolOperation`) and ignores a newer version; `ToolPath` now also refuses (`command_conflict`) the same call ID with another version. New hook `DispatcherDeps.replaySafe(tool, agent, attemptId, callId)`: the service answers from the immutable manifest (`recovery: "restart"` only; `resume`, `none`, a revoked or unknown tool are not replay-safe) and `ToolPath` writes the answer on the operation (`replaySafe`, new optional field) when the call is first made. A call left open by a restart is run again from the start only when that flag is set (and, as before, a read-only call); any other local call is settled `uncertain` and never run twice. The flag comes only from the controller-approved manifest; the extension declares none and a flag in the request is refused.

**Declarations.** `tool.discover` returns names, kinds, layers and latest versions only (paged, `next` marker, never truncated silently) and a `revision` over the visible set; descriptions, schemas and instructions come only from `tool.describe`. `DeclarationBoard` (extension side) takes a frozen snapshot when a model request opens (`openRequest`), keeps it for that request, and the next request takes a new one, so a tool registered in the middle of a request appears at the next request boundary and never in the one in flight; `textOf` gives the names-and-versions block for the volatile context; a catalogue longer than `maxTools` says so (`complete: false`). The bridge's tools themselves never change while it is loaded.

**Personal extensions.** `composeTools(bridge, personal, agentId)` is the one way to build a worker's tool list: a personal tool must be named in the agent's own namespace (`a_<id>__<name>`); the `nimblenewt` and `toolbox` namespaces, other agents', bare names and duplicates are refused; the bridge's tools are always first and a bridge with a tool missing or an extra one is refused. A personal tool receives only its arguments and its call context.

**Decisions I made.** (1) A catalogue tool is classified `unknown` by the approval policy (A07 classifies by name and none of these is listed), so its first call per exact operation is parked for review (agent review, owner under strict) and the worker makes the same call again after the decision; the owner is not asked for routine steps. Classification of released tools is T02's. (2) The agent needs a standing grant for its own namespace, `tool.<a-xxxx>.*`, to run its own tools; `personalNamespace` is derived from the principal ID, so the grant can be given when the agent is enrolled. Nothing grants it automatically yet. (3) A catalogue call is recorded as a tool operation only if it reaches the gateway; a refusal from the bridge's own checks (unknown tool, bad arguments, revoked version, host mismatch, excess access) is an error answer and is not an operation record. (4) Desk, task and message operations are authenticated, bound to the attempt and fenced, but are not tool calls of the dispatcher's path (no operation record, no pause/budget judgement); they act through services that make their own checks as the agent. (5) `recovery: "resume"` is not treated as replay-safe.

**Review round 1 changes.** (1) The schema checker no longer fails open: an exhausted check is a sticky flag, always reported as a problem even inside `not`/`oneOf`/`anyOf`, the verdict is what the check returned (a generic problem when it failed with none recorded), and `schemaProblems` checks each keyword's value kind and that a `$ref` reaches a schema (object or boolean) through own properties only. (2) A pause (L01 `PauseGuard.guard`, new required `ToolExecutionDeps.pauses`) refuses every write through the bridge (`desk.write`, `task.update`, `task.transition`, `message.send`, `tool.register`) with code `paused`; reads continue. (3) `agentOf` has its own tests (non-dispatcher, unknown attempt, inactive agent, ended and expired credential refused). (4) `ScriptLaunch.maxOutputBytes`; `ToolIdentity.kind` may be `catalog` and the replay-safe hook (now `replaySafe(tool, agent, attemptId, callId)`) answers true only for `catalog` calls the service itself is handing to the gateway, so a worker's direct gateway call naming a catalogue tool gets no flag; `requestedAccess` is asked again at script start; a call that already ended is answered from its record after its version is revoked; the declaration board keeps at most 64 open snapshots; `composeTools` documents that its id is the personal-namespace id. Lead decisions recorded: a pause stops all writes through the bridge; running a tool is governed by the contract's ceiling even though registering one is not (tested: beyond the ceiling the owner is asked); parking the first call of each exact catalogue operation until T02 classifies released tools is accepted. **Enrolment does not yet grant `tool.<a-xxxx>.*` for the agent's own namespace; that is a requirement of T06b** (the catalogue's `personalNamespace` lives above the security layer, so enrolment cannot compute it without a dependency inversion).

**Not done / limits.** (a) No real sandbox: `ScriptRunner` is a port; T06b (with H12b's launcher work) must implement it with the A03 launcher (argv and stdin as data, empty environment, the manifest limits enforced by A08b: the service passes the limits but does not enforce CPU, memory or time). (b) The extension is not yet started by a composed worker: `LocalWorker`'s `RuntimeFactory` (T06b owns this wiring; H12c landed without it) must call `boundTransport(order.credential, (token, req) => service.handle(token, gateway, req))`, `createBridge`, `composeTools`, `board.openRequest` at each model request and put `board.textOf(requestId)` in the volatile context, and pass `service.replaySafe` into `DispatcherDeps`; the integration test does this by hand for the transport, bridge, `composeTools` and `replaySafe` only (it never calls `board.openRequest` or `textOf`). (c) The adapter does not itself ask the board at a request boundary (Pi 1.3.0 has no request-boundary hook the adapter exposes; `volatileContext` is the supported route). (d) Meeting ballots, job control, environment-maintenance requests are M06, T07, A09. (e) `message.send` and `task.*` go to services that need their own deps wired by the composer. (f) The claim that a tampered in-process extension cannot call services directly is verified only for the API surface (the extension object has no service reference, and the closed request list is enforced by the controller). While the extension runs in the worker's own process, a tampered one could still reach whatever that process can; real separation needs the worker to run in its own process (follow-up T06b). The digest check of the loaded extension against the catalogue's `nimblenewt.*` version is A08/H12c's (`systemDigest`), not repeated here. (g) `tool.register` does not review the script; registering grants nothing and use goes through the policy.

**Review round 2 changes (2026-10-25).** (1) A provider's tool-call ID the controller refuses (OpenAI Responses IDs hold `|`; others are long) is sent by the extension as `h:` plus 40 hex of its SHA-256, always the same digest for the same ID; fitting IDs go unchanged; the declaration board's own call IDs likewise. **Requirement for T06b: call IDs must be unique within an attempt** (the ID is the idempotency key of `desk.write`, `message.send`, `tool.register` and tool operations, so a server that reuses `call_0` every turn would have a later write replayed as the first). (2) The output limit counts bytes. (3) The controller's own fields are spread after the caller's arguments, safe by construction. **Known limits added:** (h) `ToolPath` raises `command_conflict` for the same call ID with another tool version for every tool kind (a builtin's version is the worker's build), so a relocated or upgraded worker re-asking an open builtin call with the same ID is refused until the recovery scan settles it; (i) local placements only: a remote (H02) attempt would check the manifest against its host's facts but run the script on the controller side, the remote protocol having no bridge endpoint, so T06b must add one or refuse bridge catalogue calls from remote attempts; (j) `DeclarationBoard` takes its revision from the last page only, and `maxTools <= 0` makes `openRequest` throw.

## T06b notes, part 1: scripts run through the sandbox launcher (2026-10-25)

T06b is built in pieces; this is the first, the only one that needs neither H12b's worker process nor a wire. T06b is still open: its blocker H12b (the real worker process) is not built, and the bound transport and request-boundary wiring are T06b's own later pieces.

`packages/worker/src/sandbox/script.ts` implements the controller's `ScriptRunner` port on `SandboxLauncher` (the worker package depends on no other package, so it restates the launch it accepts as `ScriptRun`; a T06 `ScriptLaunch` is accepted as it is). Each run gets a fresh private directory (work, home, scratch) holding the pinned source at the manifest's entry point (mode 0444) and nothing else, the interpreter's install mounted read-only at `/opt/interpreter`, no network, the environment the launch carries (`ScriptLaunch.env`, empty today: manifests have no environment field) beside the launcher's own HOME, PATH and TMPDIR (and the PWD the shell of bubblewrap sets), the manifest's `cpus` and `memoryMb` as launcher limits, `limits.seconds` as the launcher's time limit, and the service's output cap as the launcher's. The model's input is delivered on standard input only; `argv` is the entry point and nothing else. The directory is removed when the run ends, whatever its outcome: a script owns what it creates and can strip its own permissions, so a failed removal is retried after the directories' permissions are restored, and a directory that still cannot be removed is reported through `onLeftover` and never replaces the run's result or reaches the model. A run that times out, is cut off at the output cap, is killed (the call's abort signal ends it), exits with an error or leaves the sandbox unclean is a failure with the reason, and the dispatcher records the operation as `failed` (the time limit and the output cap are tested through a real Pi conversation, the real service and the real launcher, `integration/src/t06b-script-runner.test.ts`, which also checks that the model is given the reason; kill by abort, error exit, a failed start, an unclean sandbox, an abort before or during the launch and the permission trick are unit tests in `script.test.ts`).

`RunResult.stdoutTruncated` says which stream was cut (a noisy standard error is not a failure). A script that writes exactly the cap to standard output is a success; one that writes more is cut off and fails. `LaunchRequest.stdin` is new: text of at most 1 MiB written to the process's standard input, which is then closed; without it the process reads end-of-file at once.

**Decisions.** (1) `requireEnforced` defaults to true: the manifest's limits must be fully enforced by the host or the run is refused with the launcher's reason; an owner who accepts a host that cannot enforce them (no systemd cgroup scope) sets it false, and the launch record still says how each limit was applied. Failing closed is the A08b rule; a host that cannot enforce limits is told so at the first run, not silently unlimited. By A08b a host without a user systemd cgroup scope enforces memory only partly, so with the default every script run is refused there (a worker run as a system service, a container); the real-process tests in this change all use `requireEnforced: false`, and the default is tested against a launcher that reports it can enforce nothing. (2) The interpreter comes from the host's own `interpreters(name, version)` lookup, never from the manifest or the caller. (3) The integration package may now depend on the worker package (test only, boundaries and lockfile updated).

**Limits.** (a) Linux (bubblewrap) is the tested path; on macOS the interpreter is run at its host path and on Windows the launcher still requires a POSIX absolute path, so a Windows host refuses the run (its own T06b follow-up with the Windows backend). (b) CPU and memory use are not read back (`ScriptResult` carries the text only), so the tool path's resource figures stay empty for script runs. (c) The script cannot reach the agent's checkout or desk; it has only its input. A tool that needs files gets them through a later card's arguments, not through a mount. (d) No process-count or disk limit is passed (manifests carry only `cpus`, `memoryMb` and `seconds`): within its run a script can start many processes or fill its private directory, bounded by the host's own limits and removed afterwards. A fixed runner default needs a host that enforces it fully and belongs with A08b's follow-ups. (e) The entry-point rule is shared in spirit with the catalogue's (relative, no `..`, no `.` segment, no `//`, no trailing `/`; the catalogue now refuses the others at registration). (f) The remaining T06b clauses (the real worker process and bound transport, request-boundary declarations, replay-safe wiring, the agent's namespace grant from enrolment, unique call IDs, remote attempts) are not done.

## H12b notes (the worker process)

- The Pi conversation of an attempt runs as its own process (`packages/pi-adapter/src/worker/main.ts`) inside the bubblewrap sandbox with no network. It speaks to the controller over one JSON-lines channel on stdin/stdout; the protocol and its validators are in `packages/contracts/src/worker-channel.ts` (version 1, 4 MiB line cap, bodies base64).
- Two kinds of request leave the process: **bridge** requests (the protected tool bridge, carrying the task credential, answered by the controller's `ToolExecutionService`) and **fetch** requests (model calls). The process replaces `globalThis.fetch` with a function that only accepts the relay's own address.
- **Relay** (`controller/src/dispatch/relay.ts`, `ModelRelay`): one narrow component on the controller side. A call is accepted only under `<relayBase>/m0/…`, goes only to the endpoint the owner configured for the attempt's route, carries only a short list of request headers, gets the provider's key from the endpoint's `headers()` (never in the sandbox), is refused on `..`, encoded separators, other methods, over-size bodies, and has the cache policy (H12e) applied around it. Only a short list of response headers is passed back. H12i (per-task network grants) will extend this same component.
- Host side (`packages/worker/src/host`): `WorkerProcess` (channel peer with in-flight limits, a protocol breach kills the process) and `localWorkerParts` (`SandboxPort` + `RuntimeFactory` shapes for `LocalWorker`, restated structurally because the worker package depends only on contracts). The turn number is kept on the host and wrapped into every checkpoint (`{pi, nn:{turnSeq}}`).
- The guest sees `/opt/node`, `/opt/app/node_modules` and `/opt/app/packages/{pi-adapter,contracts,registry}` read-only; the controller and worker packages are physically absent.
- Not done here (T06b remainder): the composer that wires the real `ToolExecutionService`, declarations at request boundaries, and the per-agent grant.

## T06b notes, part 2 (the composed worker)

- The worker runtime is now the real process of H12b: the dispatcher starts a `LocalWorker` over `localWorkerParts`; the bridge extension in the process sends every request over the channel with the task credential, and the host answers it with `ToolExecutionService.handle(token, {attemptId, call}, request)`. `integration/src/t06b-composed-worker.test.ts` runs the whole path (Pi in bubblewrap, relay to a scripted endpoint, registration and a script run through the launcher) and probes that the controller and worker packages cannot be loaded with the process's mounts.
- Each turn opens the declarations of its request (`DeclarationBoard.openRequest`) before it is submitted and sends them in the volatile context (`<context>…</context>`) after the stable prompt; the snapshot is closed when the turn ends, so a tool registered during a turn appears at the next one.
- Call IDs: the bridge in the process gives a repeated provider ID another ID (a digest of the ID and its number, so no provider ID can equal one), continuing after the IDs already in the stored conversation when an attempt resumes in a new process (Pi never re-runs an interrupted bridge call, so nothing replays under an old number). The service refuses a call ID reused in an attempt for a *different* request; a refused request frees its ID unless a copy of it was answered. It remembers up to 5000 IDs per attempt and 256 attempts, in memory; the dispatcher's durable operation records remain the guard across a controller restart.
- Every agent holds the grant `tool.<its namespace>.*` for all projects, derived where effective grants are worked out (`effectiveOf`), so it also covers agents enrolled earlier and survives an owner replacing the agent's grants; the tool gate still judges each call in the task's project. Tests that expected "no grant for one's own tool" now expect the approval path.
- `ToolExecutionDeps.isLocalHost` (required): catalogue tools run through this machine's launcher, so `tool.invoke` from an attempt on another host is refused ("not this machine") instead of running on the wrong host. A composer with only local workers answers `() => true`.
- Known: the catalogue list sent as volatile context becomes part of the stored user message, so each turn's list stays in the history (stale lists included). The cost is bounded by `maxTools` (50) per turn; trimming old ones is a later improvement.

## H12f notes (context enforcement, pause observation, message boundaries)

- `LocalWorkerOptions.guards` (`RuntimeGuards`) are plain functions the composer binds to the controller's services; `controllerGuards()` (dispatch/runtime-guards.ts) does it for `ContextEnforcementService`, `PauseService` and `AgentMessageService`. The worker holds none of the services.
- Start: a stored conversation over the resume share (`resumeDecision`, from the checkpoint's `nn.contextTokens`, an estimate the process reports at three characters a token) is not resumed; the runtime starts fresh and the agent is told why. The runtime's first call (`firstCall`: tool schema size reported by the process at ready, prompt estimated from the text) is admitted by C07b `admit`; refused, it is made smaller once if the runtime can (`compact`), else the attempt ends failed with the reasons and the service has opened the `context_overflow` blocker. A failing context check also refuses the attempt (fail closed).
- While the turn runs, on every scheduler beat: if the task is paused the attempt is observed `pausing` while any call is in flight and `paused` (boundary `finished`) once none is, and once more when the turn ends; nothing is delivered to a paused attempt. Otherwise `interrupt` messages are read and told to the agent at once, `safe-boundary` messages only when no call is in flight; they are acknowledged after being told, and carry the sender and purpose with a note that they are another party's text. A failing guard is reported through `onGuardError` and never stops the attempt.
- Pause observation uses the agent itself (`TaskCredentialService.agentOf`), not the task-scoped credential: the pause service takes a report from the task's assignee only. Messages are read as the task-scoped principal.
- The admission route key is the order's route with characters outside the key alphabet replaced by `:`.
- Observation command IDs carry an in-memory counter per attempt and state; after a controller restart a repeated state of the same attempt could be taken as a repeat of an earlier command (the earlier result is returned).
- Not covered here: compacting by the real process (`compact` is not provided by the host runtime, so a refused first call ends the attempt), and the interrupt budget/coalescing details, which stay in the message service.
- Review fixes: one watch pass at a time; a message is told once and only its acknowledgement is retried (`refused` ids stay queued); nothing is delivered after the turn; messages are fenced by a per-message delimiter with the authenticated sender (owner messages say so); acknowledgement IDs hash the sorted message IDs; observe IDs carry a per-process random part; an unknown stored size is not reported as 0 and an unknown system prompt counts as 8000 characters.
- Known gaps: `restart` (recipe change) does not re-run the resume and admission checks; a failing admission ends the attempt `failed` rather than retrying; `controllerGuards` is not yet wired by a production composer (tests only).
## H12d notes (quiescing attempts around integration, refresh, release and quarantine)

- `QuiesceCoordinator.around(scope, operation, run, after?)` (dispatch/quiesce.ts): for every live attempt of the task or agent (`DispatcherService.liveAttemptsOf`, staff only) it calls `quiesce` (freeze, settle with cancel, checkpoint), runs the operation only when all are quiet, and releases them afterwards, also when the operation fails. If one cannot be made quiet the operation does not run: a `runtime_hold` blocker on the task or agent says which attempt and why, the others are released, and the caller gets `QuiesceRefused`. An attempt that ended meanwhile has nothing to release.
- The operations live where their files are (the worker package's `GitBridge.integrate/refresh`, A06's release, quarantine) and cannot import the controller, so the composer passes them to `around`; `integration/src/h12d-quiesce-integration.test.ts` does it with the real `GitBridge.integrate` and shows the call in flight has ended before the integration starts and the attempt goes on after.
- "Settled" is what the dispatcher means by it: a local call is told to stop and ends `cancelled`; a call that ignores the stop signal is not waited for (in the sandbox the stop kills the process). There is no quarantine operation in the code yet; the coordinator is what it will call.
- Review fixes: `quiesce` settles strictly (`settleMs`, default 10 s): a call told to stop that has not ended is not taken as settled, so the operation is refused with a blocker and nothing is recovered from the ledger; a call judged before a freeze cannot begin after it (the begin step refuses a frozen attempt); an attempt that ended in between is skipped; a freeze somebody else had set is not lifted; a release that fails after the operation opens a blocker instead of reporting the operation as failed; blocker command IDs carry a per-process random part.
- Known gaps: the attempts are listed once (an attempt started for the scope during the operation is not held; the composer should hold the scope's dispatch for the duration); freezes are not per holder, so two overlapping `around` calls on one attempt can lift each other's freeze; blockers use `runtime_hold` (a type for unfinished runtime work) and are not resolved automatically by a later success.

## H12g notes (remote MCP connections through the rebinding guard)

- `RemoteMcpConnector.connect(serverId, use)` (mcp/remote.ts) is the one way the host reaches a remote MCP server. Each connection asks `McpService.remoteEndpoint` (staff only) where the server is now: a revoked or unknown server gives nothing, so a revocation blocks every new connection. It then goes through `connectPublic`: every resolved address must be public, and `use` receives the checked address, port, TLS flag and `host` (the DNS name: the TLS server name and Host header; `registryName` is only the owner's label), never a name to look up again.
- A second lookup of the name within the connection must still give only public addresses, else the connection is refused (the rebinding pattern); a different but public answer (rotating names) is accepted and `use` always gets the first, checked address. A change over a longer time is met by the next connection being checked again. `remoteEndpoint` also respects the lifecycle gate.
- There is no MCP transport in the product yet; the transport (the SDK's streamable-HTTP client) is what the composer passes as `use`, and must connect to `target.address` with `target.host` as TLS server name and Host (a pinned IPv6 address has no brackets). A call already running over an open connection when the owner revokes is not cut by this card (authorizeCall refuses the next call).

## X11 notes (typical and exhaustive test tiers)

- **Typical tier** (`npm run test:changed`, and `ci.yml` on every push to main and pull request): `scripts/test-changed.mjs` takes the files changed against a base (default the merge base with origin/main, plus uncommitted changes; CI passes the pushed range), selects the packages with changes and every package that depends on them (`scripts/affected-packages.mjs`, from the packages' `@nimblenewt/*` dependencies), builds, runs the root checks (package rules, licenses, roadmap, the scripts' own tests) and the selected packages' tests with `NIMBLENEWT_TIER=typical` (domain suites on the reference store and SQLite only) and `NIMBLENEWT_FAST=1` (leaves out the long qualification runs in `scripts/slow-tests.json`). A change outside the packages (lockfile, root `package.json`, TypeScript config, scripts, workflows) tests every package; documentation alone tests none but still runs the root checks. With no history to compare with, everything is tested.
- **Exhaustive tier** (`npm test` locally; `exhaustive.yml` in CI, run by hand or on a `v*` tag): all four backends, the qualification runs, three operating systems × two Node versions, the controller's test files split into three shards of about equal recorded run time (`scripts/shard.mjs`, `scripts/test-weights.json`, `node scripts/test-shard.mjs <i>/3`). Shard 1 also runs the root checks and every other package. This is the run whose result is evidence for card status changes and releases. On Linux each shard is rerun with the network disabled.
- The PostgreSQL and worker-thread backends are left out of the typical tier for the domain suites, and the long storage qualification runs are left out too (`NIMBLENEWT_FAST`): in the typical tier PostgreSQL is covered only by its basic suites. A change to storage should be checked with the exhaustive tier. A change to the plan documents selects pi-adapter (its capability tests read them).
- The weights are measured seconds per test file on a developer machine; regenerate them with `node scripts/test-weights.mjs` when the suite changes a lot. A file with no recorded weight counts as the median.

## H12j notes (closing the H12b worker-process gaps)

- **Reaping:** `packages/integration/src/h12j-worker-gaps.test.ts` runs the real launcher and the real child and ends the attempt three ways: completion, stop in the middle of a turn (the model never answers), and loss (the process is killed from outside). Each time the run ends within 15 seconds and the launcher reports no `releaseProblem` (it checks every descendant it saw). A paused attempt ends through the same `stop` (the dispatcher parks it with `handle.stop()`), so it is not tested separately here.
- **Provider key:** the test records every byte of the channel in both directions, standard output and error, the launch environment and every file under the checkout, home and scratch after a full turn with a tool call, and finds neither the key nor any part of it; the model endpoint did receive the key from the relay (positive control), and the task credential is what the child was given.
- **Missed pin marker:** `CachePolicies.forAttempt` now tells the marker service what each call found of the pinned prefix (`recordOutcome` on a hit, a partial hit or a miss, never awaited by Pi's answer; a failure goes to `onError`). A miss marks the marker lost, so later calls stop expecting it. The composer passes the real `MarkerService` (it has `recordOutcome`); without it nothing is recorded.
- **What the tests prove:** on Linux the launcher's own release check is a no-op (only the Windows backend has one), so "reaped" is proved by scanning `/proc` for any process that still carries the attempt's private directory on its command line (with a positive control that the scan sees the worker while it runs). The key scan guards against a regression that would put the key in the worker's configuration, files or channel; by construction the key is added on the host by the relay and never travels to the worker. Plain, base64, base64url, URL-encoded and hex forms are scanned.
- **Library level only:** nothing in production builds `CachePolicies` or passes a pin marker yet (the composer, card H12k, does); the missed-pin recording is complete in the library. Each call is told to the marker once (new command ID part per attempt object); after a miss the attempt tells it no more. A failure in the marker service goes to `onError` (default: a redacted log line) and never to Pi.
- **Not done:** the process tests run only where bubblewrap is usable (Linux); on macOS and Windows they skip. Running the worker process under Seatbelt and Windows Sandbox needs those launchers to mount the Node installation, which is future work, so the "OS-specific launchers on their CI cells" part of H12b stays open.
- Shared test support (`h12-worker-support.ts`) was extracted from the H12b test.

## H12k notes (composing the local worker host at controller start)

- `startController({ dispatch: { local, mcp } })`: `local.build({ guards })` is called while the dispatcher is composed; `guards` are `controllerGuards` bound to the services built there (`ContextEnforcementService` over the controller's retrieval, `PauseService`, `AgentMessageService`), the dispatcher principal for context admission, the attempt's own task credential for messages and the assignee (`TaskCredentialService.agentOf`) for pause observation. The `WorkerHost` it returns (the operator's `LocalWorker` over the sandbox launcher, worker process and model relay) is added to the dispatcher's workers. `local.window(order)` gives the route's context window.
- `DispatchHandle.quiesce` is the `QuiesceCoordinator` (H12d) on the dispatcher, the blocker service and the dispatcher principal. The operations it wraps (the workspace's integrate/refresh, release, quarantine) live in the worker package and are passed to `around` by whoever drives them.
- `dispatch.mcp: { resolve }` adds the MCP service and `RemoteMcpConnector` (H12g) to the handle: a remote server is reached only through it.
- `local.close` shuts down what `build` made (called by `close()` and on a failed start); `mcp.gate` is the lifecycle gate for remote endpoints.
- **Test limits:** the composition test shows the guards are built and the host is built and closed; it does not call the guards on a real attempt (swapped agent/assignee bindings would not be caught here; `runtime-guards.test.ts` covers the bindings with stubs) and, with `manual`, never ticks the stub host.
- **Not done:** an end-to-end test that starts a controller with the real sandbox launcher and worker process and runs an attempt through the composed guards (the pieces are tested together in `t06b-composed-worker`, `h12b-worker-process`, `runtime-guards` and the composition test in `start-dispatch.test.ts`, but not through `startController` with a real child); an MCP transport that connects to the checked address. H12k therefore stays planned.

## Audit of the planned-card chain (2026-10-26, read-only, Opus)

Nothing is flipped; `plan.test.mjs` also needs C06, L05 and M01 (not audited) to be implemented before the cards they block. Verdicts against each card's acceptance text:

- **Ready once their blockers flip:** H12 (partials: the owner question is only a `grant_needed` blocker until U11b; narrow/widen proven with fake handles only; no quarantine operation yet), H12c, H12h, T06, H02 (limits belong to H12a), C06b (reuse measured on simulators only; real results go to G10).
- **H12e, not ready:** nothing in production builds `CachePolicies` or a `MarkerService` and `relay.forAttempt` is called without `cache`. Fix: build them in the composer, pass the policy to the relay and `takeUsage` to the host's `cache` option, add one worker-process test on the llama simulator (small to medium).
- **C07b, not ready (blocks H12):** only `admit` and `resumeDecision` are wired. The real worker (`pi-adapter/src/worker/main.ts`) sets no `contextPolicy`/`beforeCompact`; `confirmUsage`, `changeRoute`, `ensureHandoff` and `reportOverflow` have no callers; MCP `frameResult` still uses the fixed `MCP_LIMITS` instead of `limitTool`. Medium to large.
- **L01, not ready:** `startController` builds `ConversationService` without the pause service's availability, and `Reconciler`/`StallService` are not given `pauses`. Small.
- **U12, not ready:** the agent's question reaches no owner chat (`askOwner`, `ownerQuestions`, `answerOwner` have no caller outside `messages.ts`: needs a bridge operation and a chat route). Small to medium.
- **Order of work that frees the most cards:** (1) wire C07b into the worker and dispatcher, (2) audit and flip C06, (3) wire L01 and audit L05, (4) H12e composer wiring, (5) U12 owner-question surface and audit M01.

## C07c notes (wiring C07b into the dispatcher and the worker process)

- **At the end of every attempt** the dispatcher (`#contextAtEnd`, `AttemptDispatcherOptions.onError` for its failures) tells the context service: a failed attempt is reported (`reportOverflow`: counted once per attempt, the second in a row opens the visible `context_overflow` blocker; anything that is not a context failure is not counted), a succeeded one starts the count again (`resetOverflow`), and `ensureHandoff` writes the controller's hand-off when the attempt left none (facts: the task's title, how the attempt ended; `DispatcherDeps.handoffFacts` can add effects, artifacts and the checkpoint). The composer builds the `ContextEnforcementService` for every dispatcher, not only with a local host. `resetOverflow` with nothing counted no longer fails (a command with no change reports no revision).
- **In the worker process** `ChannelStart.context` (new, validated on the channel: stream timeout 1 s to 1 h, retries 0 to 10, tool output bytes and lines, compaction timeout 1 s to 10 min, `fallback: "extractive"`) becomes Pi's context policy (`contextPolicyOf`). The extractive fallback is a summary the process writes itself (the start and the end of what is replaced, 6,000 characters at most, labelled as an extract): it cannot hang and does not ask the model that has no room. Without it Pi's own summariser runs. `LocalHostOptions.context(order)` is where the composer sets it.
- **Still open for C07b:** `confirmUsage` (the first call checked against the provider's reported size) has no caller, because the host does not yet see the first call's reported input tokens apart from the later calls of the turn; `changeRoute` (falling back to a smaller-window route) has no caller because the dispatcher does not fall back across routes mid-attempt; MCP results still use the fixed `MCP_LIMITS` rather than `limitTool`; the default hand-off facts carry no effects or artifacts. C07b stays planned.
- **Found by review and fixed:** an attempt's end reason (a worker's or model's error text) is cleaned once at `#end` (control characters, lone surrogates, empty); before, one with a tab or escape was refused by the records and the attempt was never marked ended. The context work runs before the attempt is marked finished (a crash in between redoes it; the commands are derived from the task and attempt), only for the attempt that was still the task's active one, and each step stands alone. The hand-off quotes the reason as the worker's words, not an instruction.
- **Also open:** nothing in production sets `LocalHostOptions.context` or `handoffFacts` yet; an older worker that ignores `ChannelStart.context` gives no sign that the limits are off (the channel version is unchanged).

## L01 wiring notes (the pause gates in controller start-up)

- `startController` builds one `PauseService` (`services.pauses`) and uses its `availability` for the conversation service and the review service: a paused agent (its own gate, its team chain's or the installation's) has its messages queued and is skipped as a reviewer; a message never lifts a pause. `StartOptions.availability` is now the lifecycle's other states (suspended, stopped) behind it.
- The dispatcher composer still builds its own `PauseService` for the dispatcher, messages, meetings and stand-ups (a stateless service over the same store, so the two agree).
- **Not wired:** nothing in `startController` builds the `Reconciler` or the `StallService` (they exist only in the slice harness and tests), so they have no `pauses` to be given; wiring them belongs with the reconciler loop's own composition. L01 stays planned.

## H12e wiring notes (the cache policy in the composed dispatcher)

- `dispatch.cache` (`secret`, `endpoints`, optional shared `state`, `isolationRequired`, `pinMarker(order)`) makes the composer build `CachePolicies` over a `MarkerService` on the controller's epoch (so a missed pin marks its marker lost) and hand `local.build` a `cache.forOrder(order)`: one `AttemptCache` per attempt (the same object for every ask until a minute after the attempt's expiry, then dropped; an entry is never dropped while its attempt can still run, because a second object would split the figures and the pin state), keyed by project and route. The operator's build uses `attempt.policy` for `relay.forAttempt(.., policy)` and `policy.key`/`retention` plus `takeUsage` for the host's `cache` option, so the relay's calls and the turn's reported figures are one thing.
- **Not done:** no end-to-end test starts a controller with a real worker process and shows slot partitioning through the composed path (the pieces are covered by `pi-fetch.test`, `cache.test`, `h12e-pi-cache` and the new composition test); the pin marker for a project is chosen by the operator's `pinMarker` (nothing yet creates the project's markers). H12e stays planned until the host composition (H12k's end-to-end test) exercises it.

## U12 owner-question surface notes

- State API operations `owner.ask` (an agent; needs `attempt.work`, `history.append` and `history.read` on the task's project, as the inquiry service requires), `owner.questions` and `owner.answer` (owner only) front `AgentMessageService.askOwner`, `ownerQuestions` and `answerOwner`. `startController` builds the message service for them (with the pause service's availability and the owner notifications), so the surface exists without the dispatcher. The API documentation is regenerated (`npm run api:docs:write`).
- **Not tested here:** that the answer resolves a linked typed wait through this surface (`messages.test` covers it at service level), and the owner chat client showing the list (U11b's Needs-you view). U12 stays planned until L05 and M01 are audited and flipped.
- **U12 review fixes:** `askOwner` now requires the task to be assigned to the asking agent (the project-wide `attempt.work` grant alone let any agent of the project ask); `OwnerQuestionView` carries `trust: "untrusted-content"`; a repeat of `owner.ask` after the question was answered no longer fails on the notification; the list is in the order asked within a page (pages follow the inquiry IDs, so across pages the order is not by time). Open: answering through `inquiry.answer` instead of `owner.answer` leaves the owner-question notification open.

## Audit of C06, L05 and M01 (2026-10-26, read-only, Opus)

- **None can flip yet:** C06 and L05 are blocked by C05, M01 by U03 (neither has a PROGRESS entry; U03 has notes). Every other blocker is implemented. C05 gates C06, L05, G09 and G10; U03 gates M01 and through it M02, M03, M04 and U12, so several built cards wait behind them.
- **C06:** every clause is met by tests except "tests against recorded responses": the fixtures are derived from source and documentation, not captured from running servers (G10 work, or accept the wording). It is wired in production through `pi-fetch`, `cache` and the composer.
- **L05:** its own clauses are met; in production only `guardFor` is used. Nothing calls `begin`, `record` or `complete` because the Pi adapter exposes no stream events, so revision-bound streaming exists only in the service and tests (medium; may belong to H12/T01 scope). Optional decoder resume is not built and is marked unsupported in every profile.
- **M01:** its clauses are met at service level, but nothing in production calls `MeetingService.tick`, and scheduled meetings have no `InvitationSink`: a small `meetingLoops()` (tick and `dispatchInvitations`) in the composer plus a test closes it.

## Audit of C05 and U03 (2026-10-26, read-only, Opus)

- **C05, one small fix from ready:** every clause has a service-level test in `branches/branches.test.ts` (inquiry sees permitted context with the parent unchanged, selected updates applied once including the concurrent case, suspend/switch/restore across a restart and a newer task, the return destination surviving an urgent task, a child getting only selected references). The gap is wiring: nothing outside `branches/` builds `ContextBranchService`, so `startController` and the dispatcher never open, restore or resume a branch. Fix (small): construct it in `api/start.ts`, expose open, applyUpdates, suspend, restore and resume as API operations, and test through `startController`. Carrying a restore into the Pi conversation belongs to H12/G09 (medium).
- **U03, not ready:** clear edits, ambiguity questions, untrusted text, proposals, per-project context, task pins, agent/team/contract commands, decomposition and assignment, and inquiry-only-when-records-are-insufficient are all proven (`chat/chat.test.ts`, `chat/coordinator.test.ts`). Open: (1) nothing in production builds `ChatService` or `CoordinatorService`, so the coordinator never runs (small to medium to compose; making it a hosted agent record with a lifecycle is large); (2) the only interpreter is `ScriptedInterpreter`, none is backed by a real model and the coordinator's routes are not tied to R01/R02 (medium); (3) "every dispatched task carries an explicit project and checkout" is enforced only in chat, the dispatcher does not check it (small).
- **Smallest path:** C05 wiring (small); the dispatcher guard (small); compose chat and the coordinator in `startController` with an owner-only operation and a test that the controller works with the coordinator paused, stopped or failing (small to medium); a model-backed interpreter over the R01/R02 client (medium, or split into its own card); the hosted-agent coordinator as a follow-up card (large).

## C05 and U03 wiring notes

- **C05:** `startController` builds `ContextBranchService` (`services.branches`) and the state API offers `branch.open`, `branch.get`, `branch.apply`, `branch.suspend`, `branch.switch`, `branch.resume`, `branch.pending` and `branch.delegate` (each access check is the service's own; an unknown or unreadable branch reads as missing). A test through `startController` opens two branches, suspends and switches, and reads them back. Carrying a restore into the Pi conversation during dispatch is still the dispatcher's and G09's work.
- **U03:** `StartOptions.chat` (`interpreter`, and an optional `coordinator: { model, token }`) builds `ChatService` and `CoordinatorService` (`controller.chat`, `controller.coordinator`); absent, none is built. A coordinator whose credential is missing or invalid is now `unavailable`, never an error to the caller. A test starts a controller with a scripted interpreter and a coordinator whose model fails, and shows the chat answering and the records untouched while the coordinator is paused, running-but-failing, and stopped.
- **Decision on the dispatcher guard:** "every dispatched task carries an explicit project and checkout" is met for chat-originated work (the pins). A dispatcher-level refusal of any task without a `repositoryId` would stop research and documentation tasks that have no repository; the right guard is `resolveCheckout` before a worker starts for a task that names a repository (the `checkout_changed` protection), which is a later piece of dispatch work (S02 and H12), not part of U03.
- **Split out:** U03b (first release, feeds D03) backs the chat interpreter and the coordinator with the routed model client; U03c (optional, after the first release, feeds D05) makes the coordinator a hosted agent with its own lifecycle. Neither interpreter is backed by a model yet, so U03 stays planned until U03b is built.
- **Review fixes:** the coordinator's principal is resolved once per call and reused, so a credential revoked mid-call cannot throw out of `answer`, `decompose` or `assign`; the branch operations also include `branch.restore`, `branch.redirect`, `branch.append` and `branch.history` (a single suspended branch can come back); the chat is built without the approval profile (A07), status (U05) and policy edits (U04), so every order is a proposal until they are composed, and `StartOptions.chat` says so.

## M01 wiring notes (the meeting clock)

- `meetingLoops` (`dispatch/loops.ts`) is added to the composed dispatcher's loops: `meetings.tick` (every page of schedules, up to 10 pages a tick, so a backlog after downtime is worked off over ticks) and `meetings.invite` (for each project, the invitations of every planned occurrence go to the sink; the service withholds the paused and unavailable and sends nothing for a catch-up report or a settled meeting). `DispatchStartOptions.invitations` is the sink; without it invitations are decided and recorded but nothing is sent, and nobody provides a production sink yet (the host decides how an agent is reached).
- M01 stays planned: its blocker U03 is not implemented.

## Chat composition: policy, status, policy edits

- `startController` with `chat` now builds `PolicyService`, `StatusService` and `PolicyEditService` and gives them to `ChatService`, so the approval profile (A07), status explanations (U05) and conversational policy edits (U04) apply instead of every order being a proposal. The stall service is not passed to status (L01 leaves it unbuilt at start), so stall concerns are not shown there.

## C07b: MCP results framed as data

- A confirmed MCP tool call's text is now returned (and summarised on the operation) as: a notice that it is output from the external server and is data, a warning line when it contains phrases that read as instructions, the text scrubbed of credentials and cut at `MCP_LIMITS.text` (20,000 characters), and a note when cut. Test: a long answer containing "ignore all previous instructions" is flagged, cut and bounded.
- Still open: the limit is fixed, not scaled to the model's window, and the cut part is not spilled for paging (`limitTool` needs the window of the attempt's route, which the tool service does not hold).

## U03b (part): the model-backed chat interpreter; C07b: context limits reach the worker

- `ModelInterpreter` (`chat/model-interpreter.ts`) is a `ChatInterpreter` over a `ModelCall` port (`complete(route, {system, user})`). The prompt separates the owner's words from fenced quoted material (a fence inside quoted text is broken), lists the operations, and tells the model to report instructions found in quotes as `basis: "quoted"`. Routes are read at each message and tried in order; an answer that is not a valid intent list counts as that route failing. All failing: `unavailable`, which the chat already turns into "unavailable, nothing done". U03b stays planned: there is no concrete `ModelCall` yet (the routed client with R02's failure classes, budget and effect rules is not built for controller-side calls), and the recorded-model scenarios of the card are not written.
- `local.build` now receives `context(order)`: tool output cut to the window's share (`toolOutputChars`) and the extractive compaction fallback. The operator passes it as `LocalHostOptions.context`. An integration test over the real sandbox and worker process shows the start message carries it. Open: nothing in the shipped composition builds the host, so the operator still has to pass it through.
- **Review fixes (MCP framing, interpreter, limits):** MCP text is redacted before it is cut; a replay is framed again from the stored summary; a failed MCP call's error text carries the data notice; the framing ends with a closing line; the tool-output limit is clamped to the channel's bound; the interpreter treats a reply with no list as "no intents" (so a refusal does not send the same prompt to a weaker route), and fences proposals, the pending question and the project name.

## C07b close-out, and the C06 and L05 decisions

- **First call checked against the provider's count (C07b):** `ModelRelay.forAttempt({ onFirstUsage })` reads `prompt_tokens` or `input_tokens` from the first successful reply (the first and last 16 KB of it; the reply passes on unchanged; a failing reporter or a failed call changes nothing) and reports it with the request's length. The composer hands `local.build` `confirmFirstCall(order, usage)`, which records it against the attempt's latest admitted first call (`confirmUsage`, which calibrates the route). The operator passes it as `onFirstUsage` when it builds the relay; tests cover the relay (OpenAI-style plain and streamed, Anthropic-style streamed, no count, first call only) and the guard.
- **Split to C07d (feeds D03):** the route change to a smaller window (`changeRoute`) has no caller because nothing in the dispatcher changes a running attempt's route; and MCP results use one fixed bound (20,000 characters) rather than the window's limit with a spill. Both clauses moved to the new card C07d with their acceptance text; they are not dropped.
- **C06:** "tests against recorded responses" is now written as responses simulated from each backend's documentation or source (that is what the tests use); capturing responses from running servers and re-testing the profiles against them is added to G10, which already measures the real backends. Decision for the owner, recorded in `build-decisions-for-owner.md`.
- **L05:** the service meets its own clauses and `guardFor` is used in production. Feeding the model's stream into `begin`/`record`/`complete` needs stream events the Pi adapter does not expose, so it is the new card L05b (feeds G09). Optional decoder resume stays unsupported and is qualified separately, as the card says.
- **Review fixes (first-call count):** `local.build` also receives `attemptRelay(order, relay, { model?, cache? })`, one relay per attempt with the first-call count already going to `confirmFirstCall` (operators use it instead of `relay.forAttempt`, which made a new "first call" for every request); a token-count probe is not the call; the count is reported only when the reply ended normally and the number is complete; Anthropic-style counts add the cache read and creation tokens; a count above four windows or under half a character a token is not recorded; the calibration keeps a decayed higher earlier ratio (80%); the admission round is remembered only for an admitted call and forgotten once used.

## U03b (part): the chat's model call; second-round review fixes

- `HttpModelCall` (`chat/http-model.ts`) is a `ModelCall` over an OpenAI-compatible endpoint (the owner's endpoint configuration, credential read at each call): one unstreamed completion, no randomness, a 1 MB reply bound, a time limit. A failure is thrown with R02's class (credit, rate-limit, outage, auth, ...) and a short redacted text; `ModelInterpreter` falls back to the next route. U03b stays planned until the routed budget and effect rules of R02 are applied to these calls and the recorded-model scenarios are written; the pieces (interpreter, model call) now exist.
- **Fixes after the second review:** a replayed MCP answer says when only the first part of the earlier answer is kept; a restricted result's placeholder is not framed as server output; output bytes are those of the raw text; the "error text from the external MCP server" notice goes only on errors the executor reported; the per-attempt relay entry expires even when the order's expiry does not parse; the test that claimed to cover the impossible-count check now reaches it.

## L01 close-out and the H12 audit (2026-10-26)

- `startController` builds `StallService` with the pause lookup (a task under a pause is assessed as paused, never stalled) and gives it to the status service and to `services.stall`. Nothing runs its `scan`, and nothing builds the `Reconciler`, in production: the dispatcher principal holds no grants and `scan` needs the recovery-read right everywhere, which widens what a controller service may read; whether to give the dispatcher that right or a narrower scan right is an owner decision (build-decisions-for-owner.md). The work is the new card L01b (feeds D03); L01's own clauses (pause gates, availability hook, settlement, restart) are met by the services, the composition and their tests.
- **H12 audit:** the dispatcher, the credential issue, the tool gate, the ledger, quiesce, the pause and message guards, the cache and the MCP guard are composed and tested (the quiesce coordinator is built, but no production code calls its `around` yet). Two clauses depend on other cards, not on more H12 code: the owner question for a missing grant is a `grant_needed` blocker until U11b makes it a Needs-you item, and the real-launcher restart from a checkpoint is shown by a new integration test (`h12j-worker-gaps.test.ts`: the first process ends, a new turn in the second process reaches the model with the first answer in front of it, and the provider key is not on either channel). It does not show the freeze and settle before the restart, a recipe that is actually narrower, or the notice to the agent on widening; those stay with the fake-handle tests of the dispatcher. L01's clause that a paused agent keeps its tasks rests on `PauseService.assertRetained`, tested but not yet called by any reassignment path; that call is part of L01b. The note that no quarantine operation exists was out of date: `worker/src/sandbox/quarantine.ts` exists and `quiesce.around` accepts a quarantine operation; the composition that wraps one around the other is not built (nothing in production calls `around` or `quarantine`).

## T07 notes: durable job records and job control (2026-10-26)

`packages/controller/src/jobs/jobs.ts` (`JobService`, `controller.services.jobs`), `jobs.test.ts` (10 tests on each of the four backends). New rights `job.write` (the host or worker records a job), `job.control` (stop, reconcile, decide after a restart) and `job.read`; new `PauseService.backgroundDenied` and `McpService.usable`.

- **The handle.** The job ID is derived from the task and the caller's operation ID, so asking for the same operation twice (a retry, a restart of the worker) is one job: the same command ID answers `replayed`, a new command for the same operation is refused as already admitted. The record lives in storage, so a new controller process reads it (tested with a second service over the same store).
- **State machine.** admitted, dispatched, running, completed, failed, cancellation_requested, cancelled, outcome_unknown, with the allowed moves in one table; history keeps the last 32 moves. Output is bounded (the last 8 KB kept, 64 KB at a time, secrets redacted on the way in, the byte count kept). Logs and artifacts are artifact IDs (not checked for existence here).
- **Resumability.** Stated at admission, never implied: `none`, `tool_checkpoint` (only a tool can leave one) and `remote_tracked` (only a remote job or MCP operation). A restart from a checkpoint is refused for anything else.
- **Three stop operations.** The kind decides: a model request is cancelled, a tool is stopped, a remote job (and an MCP operation) is stopped remotely, each recorded as its own operation. The request and the host's confirmation are separate; an uncooperative stop is recorded and the job stays in cancellation_requested; orphans the stop could not end are recorded on the job. The process-tree termination itself is the sandbox launcher's (A03: process groups on POSIX, job objects on Windows); binding its result to `confirmCancel` is T07b.
- **Pause.** A foreground job is not admitted for a paused task. A permitted background job is, unless a gate on the task's chain said `background: deny`; reports and results are always accepted; a finished background job is handed to `PauseService.recordBackground` (a command derived from the job, so repeating the finish repeats nothing) and the pause stays. The principal that finishes the job needs the right to record a background result.
- **After a restart.** `live` lists the jobs that were not finished (tracked exceptions are left out); `decide` completes one from stated evidence, cancels it, restarts it from its tool checkpoint, or keeps it as a tracked exception under a named owner.
- **Outcome unknown.** A job whose result is ambiguous holds in `outcome_unknown` (no report, no finish, no cancel) until `reconcile` records the evidence, in a record of its own; `unresolved(task)` lists what a dependent action must wait for.
- **MCP.** An MCP operation is a job with a server ID. Admission asks a `JobConnections.usable` port; `startController` binds none (there is no MCP client), so no MCP job is admitted in production yet; `McpService.usable` is the intended binding.
- **After the first review (all fixed):** a finished background job is recorded through `PauseService.recordFinishedJob`, which needs no `assignment.manage` (a worker holding only `job.write` would otherwise commit its finish and be refused the pause record); a repeated `admit` is answered from its record whatever has changed since (the task checks run inside the command); `decide` cannot settle an outcome-unknown job (only `reconcile`, with evidence, or a tracked exception); output is redacted again over the joined tail (a secret split across reports), NUL is dropped, lone surrogates repaired and the cut never starts inside a character (the 8,192-character limit counts characters, not bytes); orphan descriptions are redacted, never refused; logs and artifacts must exist and belong to the job's project; Force Stop is a recorded step of its own (`requestCancel` with `force`, once, as the escalation of a stop that was not obeyed or asked for at once); reconciliations are listed from the job; a missing task reads as forbidden to a stranger; only a lost revision race is retried. **Second review:** the rest of a secret that began in an earlier report is removed from the next one; `recordFinishedJob` checks itself (the reference must be a background job of that task that has ended); `admit` locks the pause gates in its commit; artifact references are checked inside the command. **Fourth review:** private-key blocks that are open at the end of a report are dropped through their END line across reports (header split, last block of several, bounded at 16 KiB). Known limit: an END line that is itself split over two reports is not recognised, so up to 16 KiB of later output is dropped. Known limit: with a pause that denies background work, a worker's finish commits and the pause record is refused on every retry until the pause is resumed.
- **Not done (T07b):** the agent-facing job tools of the bridge, the launcher result bound to the stop confirmation, the MCP client and its connection check bound in `startController`, state API reads of jobs, running the restart decisions at controller start (nothing calls `live` and `decide` by itself) and relaunching a tool from its checkpoint (`restart` only records the decision and moves the job to dispatched). Limits: the list operations scan the job records of the store (a page of 1,000 at a time); concurrent reports on one job meet a revision check and are retried.

## U03b notes

- `chat/routed-model.ts` is the routed client for the controller's own model calls (owner chat interpreter, coordinator). `chatRoutes(routing, {role,...})` reads the routing table at each call and returns the eligible routes in order (a role with no profile or no table has none, so the chat reports itself unavailable and does nothing). `GuardedModelCall` wraps any `ModelCall`: credit/auth failures block the provider's routes until `unblock`; a rate limit cools a route for 60 s, an outage for 300 s; other failures affect that call only. The calls carry no tools and change nothing, so trying the next route repeats no effect.
- `HttpModelCall.completeDetailed` returns the endpoint's own token counts when they are whole numbers; `ChatSpendService` keeps the owner's optional daily token cap (`chat-budget`) and the UTC day's count (`chat-spend`). At the cap no call is made. Calls in flight together can overshoot by their own size. Cost in money is not computed here; routes' prices belong to R03.
- `ModelCoordinatorModel` builds the prompt for answer/decompose/assign, fences record and task text so it cannot close the fence, and returns the JSON object found in the reply; the coordinator still validates every field. The coordinator takes `routes` (called at each question) and `agents` (the active agents shown for assignment).
- The coordinator lists the agents it may assign to as its own principal, so it needs `agent.read`; without it no assignment is proposed (the coordinator never errors for it). A failure to count a paid call is reported to `onSpendFailure` and the reply is still used, so the next route never repeats a paid call. The prompt fence breaks up any run of three or more bracket characters.
- Not done: wiring `GuardedModelCall`/`chatRoutes` from configuration in the controller's start-up command line, a `chat.budget` right in the state API, and measured prompt-reading speed per route (V01b).

## F04k notes

- `startController` checks the file systems of the registered projects' checkouts at every start: after the storage is open and the epoch taken it lists the projects, takes each repository location that is a local absolute path (a URL is not a workspace), and runs the F04h filesystem check on the ones not already named in `workspaceRoots`. The decision is made again with those problems added to the earlier ones: refused with the F04h message and switch unless the override was given, and then printed once more (only the new lines) and audited in the one `environment.override` record. A refused start through this path has already opened the storage and taken the epoch; it releases both (the surrounding try/catch closes them).
- Archived projects are skipped. Cost: the check runs once per distinct checkout path, one detection each (on macOS and Windows that is a process per path, and the naming probe writes and removes one small directory inside each existing checkout); a fleet of thousands of checkouts would want detection per mount point. `Controller.filesystems` lists the checkout reports too.
- Limit: a project registered after start is not checked until the next start; a checkout created at a path that does not exist yet is judged by its nearest existing directory.

## R05b notes

- The dispatcher holds an attempt's allowance before it starts (`#reserve`, just before the worker is started, after the place is held): for each dimension in which the task's budget has a limit (a limit of 0 states none), the smaller of what the attempt may use (the contract's `maxMinutes` or the attempt's own expiry in milliseconds for compute; `reserveCostMicros` for money, default none) and what the task still has available. The reservation's command ID derives from the attempt, so a repeat after a crash finds it; the reservation ID and a running tally of the attempt's use are kept on the attempt record. A task with no budget holds nothing; a task whose limited allowance has none free is not started (check `budget`, tried again at the next tick; its place is given back).
- While a reservation is held, a turn's cost and compute are added to the attempt's tally and **settled once when the attempt ends** (the hold already counts against the task); the turn's cache figures are still recorded turn by turn. A use above the hold is recorded by the budget service as overrun. When the worker was lost (lease ran out, or the attempt expired) the hold is marked **uncertain** instead and stays at its full amount until someone settles it with what actually happened. A controller-side Stop of a live attempt settles to the tally.
- Not done: the production start does not set `reserveCostMicros` (no money is held unless an owner sets it), and nothing reconciles an uncertain hold by itself; a person or a later reconciler calls `settle`. The three ceilings agree by taking the smallest of the contract's time, the attempt's expiry and the free allowance for compute; R02's cumulative budget is a ceiling of the routing layer and is not read here.
- **After review:** the amounts to hold are written to the attempt (`reserving`) before the budget is asked, so a repeat after a crash (or a second dispatcher) asks for the same thing and finds the same hold; usage reports are added to the tally once per key (the last 200 keys are kept with a digest; a different report under the same key is refused with `command_conflict`); an attempt that holds nothing yet and finds none of a limited allowance free (held by other work or by a lost attempt's uncertain hold) opens the budget blocker instead of waiting silently; only a lost lease marks the hold uncertain (an expiry the controller itself ended settles to the tally). Known limit: a lost attempt's tally is not settled, its whole hold stays uncertain until reconciled by hand.

## F04j notes (sandbox and limits as start-up prerequisites)

- A controller that builds a local worker host (`dispatch.local`) now requires the sandbox tools and the limits mechanism at start, through the one prerequisite list and its message (what was found, what is required, how to install it, and that a missing tool cannot be overridden). `StartOptions.features` still says it explicitly (a test, or a controller whose workers are all remote). The launcher's own health check remains the second line: it refuses to advertise capacity if the tools disappear later.

## U03c notes (the coordinator through the owner's chat)

- A new intent `coordinator` carries what the owner says to the coordinator: `answer` (a question, answered from the records with citations, or an inquiry when the records are insufficient), `decompose` (an outcome, drafted as a proposal that exists only after the owner applies it), `assign` (hand out the open tasks: proposed, or made if the coordinator holds the right), `configure` (its principal and up to four routes) and `start` / `pause` / `stop`. The text fields must be the owner's own words (the same check as any command), the project is the one named or in focus, and each item is applied once under a command ID derived from the turn. The chat reaches the coordinator through a lookup (`ChatDeps.coordinator`) because the coordinator is built after it; with none, the item is refused as unavailable.
- The coordinator lists the active agents under its own grants (needs `agent.read`; without it the list is empty and nothing is proposed for assignment). The composed-controller test covers set-up and start through chat, an answer with a cited desk record, a drafted plan applied by a later message, assignment with the agent list shown to the model, a text the owner did not say, and pausing.
- Not done: a state-API operation for the coordinator's lifecycle (it is reached by chat and by the in-process handle), and production configuration of the chat and the routed model at start (U03b notes).

## H05b notes (reviewer routing in the running controller)

- The dispatcher's tick now runs `reviews.sweep` for every project (`reviewLoops`, dispatch/loops.ts): a waiting review takes a reviewer once one is eligible; a review whose reviewer is held, paused, stopped or past the deadline moves on under the project policy, ending with the owner. Nothing is approved by waiting.
- `ReviewService.sweep` accepts the controller's own service principal (a service with no task scope) without a project grant, as the other dispatcher loops do; every other caller still needs `review.policy.write`. That principal can neither decide a review nor set the policy (tested).
- `start.ts` binds `reviewerHost` to `dispatch.hostOfAgent`: the host of the agent's live attempt (first found). A lost host moves the review at once; a silent one waits. Without a live attempt the host is not judged (`unknown`).
- The host is the running attempt's host or, for an attempt that was moved, the host it is pinned to; a failing lookup means the host is not judged. Cost: each judgement scans the live attempts (no per-tick memo yet).
- Known limits: `wakeList` still has no caller (waking a reviewer is the attempt admission path); `hostStaleAfterMs` keeps its default.

## L01b notes (reconciler and stall detection in the running controller)

- New action `recovery.stall-scan` (security/access.ts). `StallService.scan` accepts the owner, a service holding `recovery.stall-scan` on "*", or a service holding `recovery.read` on "*"; nothing else (a project-scoped grant, a bare service and agents are refused; tested on all four backends). The right permits the scan only: it gives no read of any record and is not the broad right.
- `recoveryLoops` (dispatch/loops.ts) adds two loops to the dispatcher's tick: `recovery.reconcile` (the `Reconciler` pass) and `recovery.stall-scan` (paged scan). A dispatcher that lacks the right is told to the owner once per epoch as a maintenance notice (`stall-off:<epoch>`, coalesced with the other dispatcher notice) and the scan is skipped, so the tick does not fail each time. The owner grants `recovery.stall-scan` to `service:dispatcher` at set-up.
- `start.ts` builds the `Reconciler` with the pause lookup (an inbox message for a paused task stays received), the wait service and the inbox/effect services. Delivery destinations and effect bridges are not built yet, so `destinations` and `bridges` are empty: it repairs task events, admits inbox messages, recovers effects (reports unbridged ones) and settles waits.
- The scan reads the dispatcher principal again each tick, so a right granted or revoked while the controller runs takes effect at the next tick (no restart); its cursor is kept between ticks and wraps, so every assignment is reached however many there are; a notice that failed is tried again.
- Known limits: reassignment through the pause service's retention check still has no caller; the acceptance sentence "a paused task is not reported stalled" rests on the existing `isPaused` option of the stall service (tested in health/stall.test.ts), and the composed test proves the loops and the right, not a time-driven stall.

## H12g and U05 composed tests (2026-10-27)

- H12g: `start-dispatch.test.ts` now proves, through `startController`, the rebinding pattern (public at the check, private at the second lookup: refused, transport never called), a rotating public name (first checked address used) and revocation (no new connection), beside the private-address and pause cases that were there. No MCP transport exists yet (T05).
- U05: `api/start-status.test.ts` proves through `startController` that the owner chat answers a task question, an overview and the noticeboard from the records, every line cites a record, nothing is written, and an unknown task is not answered. The service-level detail (freshness, blockers kinds, tools used) stays proven in `chat/status.test.ts`.
- U05 follow-up (same run): `start.ts` now builds the `WatchService` (event watches) and gives it to the composed `StatusService` (`watch`, so the failed event queue can be listed in the running controller (inspecting a seeded failure is proven at service level only: status.test.ts, watchers.test.ts)) and to the state API (`watches`, so the `events.watch*` operations are served). The composed status test also asks for changes, tools, delivered and the failed queue. Artifact verification (`verifyArtifact`) still needs a blob store the controller does not build, so delivered artifacts show `not_checked`: card U05b. The non-owner, permission-filtered answers are proven at service level (the chat is the owner's).
